## Milestone 3: decisions deferred to Opus 5.5 (2026-10-11)

Deferred under D-007 and logged **before** any milestone 3 code, test or test case exists. The owner asked specifically that the agreement tolerance and the horizon go to Opus 5.5 before they are locked (D-041). Reviewer: Opus 5.5 (subagent `a6ca5197-6ba5-4ead-9e23-87e5e2d7d9eb`), in two exchanges: the design review (proposals P1 to P8 and questions Q9 to Q11) and a follow-up on three lab findings (D-044, D-046, D-047). Both replies returned the full verdict block. Each entry shows the resulting choice and what Opus 5.5 changed from the implementer's recommendation. D-025 applies from this commit: every value below is predeclared, and any later change goes to Opus 5.5 first. Two of Opus 5.5's conditions were mechanical and were run in the lab before this commit: the 20-sample tail check (D-046) and the calibration of the nonlinear ceilings (D-044).

**Evidence behind the review: uncommitted pre-freeze calibration, not results.** Throwaway scripts in `/tmp/m3lab` (a prototype of the same classical RK4 and SciPy DOP853 on the committed `vector_field`; arbitrary calibration seeds 31415, 27182, 16180, 99991 and the streams `[4242, k]` and `[4243, k]`, none of them a gate stream; run on the laptop). They are not committed because they only informed design choices; every milestone 3 result will come from the committed tests and script. Errors are the maximum absolute value over state components and output times. "Chaotic-ish" means `E > 0` (above the pivot-height zero of `V`) released at rest; it is not a classification of chaos, which is milestone 6. What the lab showed, besides the numbers given in the entries:
- **Reference accuracy.** DOP853 at `rtol = atol = 1e-12` versus `1e-13` differs by about 2e-12 on regular states (`T ≤ 10`). On 30 chaotic-ish states the difference is at most 2.3e-11 at `T = 5` and 1.35e-9 at `T = 10`; on 6 states at `T = 20` it is 8e-8. So the reference's own error grows roughly exponentially with the horizon in chaotic states. SciPy silently raises any `rtol` below 2.22e-14 to 2.22e-14, with a `UserWarning`, so `1e-14` is not available. Interpolation at `t_eval` is not a visible error source (`max_step = 0.01` changes results by about 2e-12).
- **Parameter dependence.** With ten non-default parameter sets in unscaled time, the RK4-versus-DOP853 error at `dt = 0.01`, `T = 10` reaches 2.8e-3 (30 chaotic-ish states). With `dt` and `T` stated in units of `t_s = √(L1/g)` and the error measured on `(θ1, θ2, ω1 t_s, ω2 t_s)`, it reaches 2.1e-5. So times are stated in `t_s` and errors on the nondimensional state (derivation note, section 8).
- **Cost.** One jitted right-hand-side call from NumPy takes about 15 µs; DOP853 at `1e-12` over `t ∈ [0, 10]` took about 2,700 evaluations and 0.06 s for one state; the 20 × 30 solves of D-046 took 38 s. The CI runner has been 1 to 2.5 times slower than the laptop (the 409-test suite took 35 s to 90 s on near-identical code).

### D-042 Public interface and units of `horizon.integrators` (P1, AMEND)
- **Choice:** `src/horizon/integrators.py`, with the conventions of D-028: one state per call, a state array of shape `(n,)`, float64, `params` a required argument, an autonomous vector field `f(state, params) -> dstate` of the same shape, and batching by the caller with `jax.vmap` (over `state0`, and optionally over `params` as a `Params` of arrays). Time is in whatever unit `f` uses; for the pendulum that is `√(L1/g)`, the nondimensional unit of section 8 of the derivation note. The docstrings state shapes and units. The functions are generic over `f`, so the exact-solution problems of D-044, Lorenz and the tangent dynamics of milestone 5 reuse the same code.
  - `rk4_step(f, state, params, dt) -> Array`, shape `(n,)`: one classical RK4 step.
  - `rk4_trajectory(f, state0, params, dt, n_out, stride) -> Array`, shape `(n_out + 1, n)`: row `k` is the state at `t = k · stride · dt`, and row 0 is `state0`. `f`, `n_out` and `stride` are static (`n_out` and `stride` are Python ints ≥ 1, checked in Python, which is allowed because they are static); `dt`, `state0` and `params` are traced; the function is jit-compiled. No times array is returned: the docstring defines `t_k = k · stride · dt` as an integer multiple, never accumulated. Because `f` is static, a new lambda or `functools.partial` on each call recompiles, and the docstring says so.
  - `solve_reference(f, state0, params, t_eval, *, rtol=1e-12, atol=1e-12) -> numpy.ndarray`, shape `(len(t_eval), n)`, float64: `scipy.integrate.solve_ivp` with `method="DOP853"` (the values of brief §3 are the defaults). `f` is jitted once inside and adapted to `f(t, y)`. It integrates over `t_span = (0, t_eval[-1])`, requires `t_eval[0] >= 0` and `t_eval` strictly increasing, raises `RuntimeError` if `solve_ivp` reports a non-zero status, and returns `np.asarray(..., dtype=np.float64)`. The `solve_ivp` call runs inside `warnings.catch_warnings()` with `warnings.simplefilter("error")`, so SciPy's silent clamp of `rtol` raises instead of quietly changing the tolerance.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** the `t_span` and `t_eval` requirements and the float64 return; the warnings-as-errors wrapper around `solve_ivp`; the docstring note about the static `f`.
- **Implementer note:** `simplefilter("error")` turns *any* warning raised while `solve_ivp` runs into an exception, not only the `rtol` clamp. That is stricter than the clamp alone; if an unrelated warning ever appears, it is reported, not silenced.
- **Alternatives:** pendulum-specific wrappers; returning `(times, states)`; returning a SciPy dense-output object; a convenience batched function.
- **Why:** one generic surface keeps the fast integrator and the reference interchangeable. The clamp is exactly the kind of silent tolerance change that D-025 exists to prevent.

### D-043 RK4 scheme (P2, APPROVE)
- **Choice:** classical RK4 (stages at 0, ½, ½, 1; weights ⅙, ⅓, ⅓, ⅙) in two nested `lax.scan`s, the outer over outputs and the inner over `stride`, so that only the output rows are stored. `dt` is not validated: a negative `dt` is legal and is needed for the reversibility test of milestone 4.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** nothing.
- **Why:** it is minimal, and nothing in the validation suite needs more.

### D-044 Exact-solution gate (P3, AMEND; follow-up CONFIRM)
- **Choice (no pendulum and no DOP853 involved):** four initial-value problems with a closed-form solution, each integrated with RK4 on the grid `dt ∈ {0.04, 0.02, 0.01, 0.005}`, with outputs at the integer times `t = 0, 1, …, T`. The statistic is the maximum over the output times and components of `|RK4 − exact|`.

  | Problem | Equation | `y(0)` | `T` | Ceiling at `dt = 0.005` | Calibrated |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | harmonic oscillator | `ẋ = v`, `v̇ = −4x` | `(1, 0.5)` | 10 | 3e-8 | 2.7e-9 |
  | linear, non-normal | `ẏ = A y`, `A = [[−1, 5], [0, −2]]` | `(1, −1)` | 5 | 2e-9 | 1.1e-10 |
  | logistic | `ẏ = y (1 − y)` | `0.1` | 5 | 7.5e-12 | 7.4e-13 |
  | cubic rotation | `ẋ = −(x² + y²) y`, `ẏ = (x² + y²) x` | `(1, 0.5)` | 5 | 1.1e-8 | 1.1e-9 |

  The cubic rotation keeps its radius and turns at the rate `x0² + y0²`; the derivation note will give all four exact solutions. **Gate, for each problem:** (i) the least-squares slope of `log(error)` against `log(dt)` over the four grid points is in **[3.9, 4.1]** (calibrated 4.00, 4.03, 3.99, 4.00); (ii) the error at `dt = 0.005` is at most the ceiling (10 to 18 times the calibrated value); (iii) the DOP853 wrapper at its default tolerances is within **1e-10** of the exact solution (calibrated 4.6e-12, 9.0e-13, 1.05e-12, 5.1e-12).
- **Lab evidence, uncommitted, not results: which problems expose a wrong RK4.** Least-squares slopes over the grid for the classical scheme and three deliberately wrong variants. W1: `k3` evaluated at `y + dt/2 · k1` instead of `k2`. W2: weights `(0, ⅓, ⅓, ⅓)` with every stage fraction ½, which is fourth order on linear constant-coefficient problems and second order in general. W3: `k4` evaluated at `y + dt/2 · k3` instead of `y + dt · k3`.

  | Problem | classical | W1 | W2 | W3 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | harmonic oscillator | 4.00 | 2.00 | **4.00** | 1.03 |
  | linear | 4.03 | 2.02 | **4.03** | 1.02 |
  | logistic | 3.99 | 2.00 | 2.00 | 0.99 |
  | cubic rotation | 4.00 | 2.04 | 2.06 | 1.01 |

  The two linear problems cannot see W2; both nonlinear problems can.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** added a nonlinear exact problem. On linear constant-coefficient problems, any scheme whose stability polynomial equals RK4's has the same error, so a wrong stage placement passes both linear problems (W2 is the worked example). In the follow-up, Opus 5.5 kept **both** nonlinear problems, the logistic it had approved and the cubic rotation the implementer added, with the ceilings above. **Correction to Opus 5.5's first wording, logged at its request:** "scalar conditions coincide with systems for order ≤ 4" was too strong. For an autonomous scalar problem the two order-4 trees `[[τ], τ]` and `[[τ, τ]]` have the same elementary differential, `f'' f' f²`, so the logistic problem checks only the combination of their two conditions; the cubic rotation is the genuine two-dimensional check. The logistic ceiling is still meaningful, because RK4 is compared with the closed-form solution and not with the wrapper (RK4's error at `dt = 0.005`, 7.4e-13, is below the wrapper's own 1.05e-12).
- **Alternatives:** the logistic only, as first approved; the rotation instead of the logistic.
- **Why:** an exact solution needs no reference, so this gate tests RK4's order independently of DOP853 and of the pendulum. The nonlinear problems are needed because linear ones are blind to a wrong tableau that keeps the stability polynomial.

### D-045 Pendulum convergence gate (P4, APPROVE)
- **Choice:** default parameters; 12 regular states with `ω = 0` and `θ1, θ2` uniform in `[−0.4, 0.4]`, drawn as `rng.uniform(−0.4, 0.4, size=(12, 2))` from `np.random.default_rng([CASES_SEED, 2000])`; `T = 10` with outputs at `t = 0, 1, …, 10`; reference `rtol = atol = 1e-13` **for this study only** (the wrapper's default stays `1e-12`); grid `dt ∈ {0.04, 0.02, 0.01, 0.005}`. Per state the statistic is the maximum over the output times and the four components of `|RK4 − reference|`, and the slope is the least-squares slope of `log(error)` against `log(dt)` over the four points. **Gate: every state's slope is in [3.8, 4.2].**
- **Validity rule, so the test cannot pass at the reference's noise floor:** the error at `dt = 0.005` must be at least **30 times the reference's self-error**, the maximum absolute difference between the `1e-13` reference and a `2.5e-14` reference on the same output grid (`2.5e-14` is just above SciPy's clamp of 2.22e-14). If the rule is violated, the test **fails as inconclusive**; it never passes.
- **Calibration, uncommitted:** slopes 3.919 to 3.997 (pairwise 3.85 to 4.02) and a floor ratio of at least 81. The finer grid `{0.02, 0.01, 0.005, 0.0025}` was excluded because its floor ratio fell to 4. A moderate-amplitude state outside this set, `(0.5, −0.3)`, has pairwise slopes of 3.76, 3.67, 3.66, 3.86 and 3.93 over `dt = 0.1` down to 0.003; RK4's own self-convergence, `‖y(dt) − y(dt/2)‖` with no reference, shows the same dips (3.85, 3.68, 3.64, 3.85), so they are genuine pre-asymptotic behaviour and not reference error. That is why the amplitude bound and the grid are declared in advance and not tuned afterwards. With ten non-default parameter sets in unscaled time the slopes ranged from 3.53 to 4.05 and the floor ratio fell to 9, which is why this gate uses the default parameters only; time-scaled non-default sets are exercised by D-047. The wrong variants of D-044 give slopes of 1.97 to 2.00 (W1), 1.93 to 2.00 (W2) and 1.00 to 1.04 (W3) on these states.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** nothing. Its note: at `|θ| ≤ 0.4` the dynamics are nearly linear, so this test alone would be weak against nonlinear order defects, which is why D-044 carries the nonlinear checks (the lab shows that W2 is nevertheless caught here).

### D-046 Agreement gate: tolerance and horizon (P5, AMEND; follow-up CONFIRM). The owner's flagged item
All times are in units of `√(L1/g)`; default parameters; `dt = 0.01`.
- **(a) Chaotic-ish states.** 30 states with `ω = 0` and `θ1, θ2` uniform in `[−π, π]`, kept only if `E > 0` (per attempt `rng.uniform(−π, π, size=2)`, repeated until 30 are kept), from `np.random.default_rng([CASES_SEED, 2001])`. **Horizon `T = 8`**, outputs at `t = 0, 1, …, 8`. Reference `rtol = atol = 1e-12` (the brief's). Statistic: the maximum over states, output times and components of `|RK4 − DOP853|`. **Tolerance `τ_chaos = 1e-4`.**
- **(b) Regular states.** The 12 states of D-045, `T = 50` with outputs at every integer time, reference `1e-13`, **`τ_regular = 1e-6`**. Uncommitted calibration of exactly this design: 20 disjoint samples of 12 states (streams `[4243, k]`, `k = 0..19`) gave sample maxima of 2.1e-8 to 2.4e-8 (no heavy tail), and the 12 convergence-calibration states (seed 99991) gave 2.3e-8; the margin is about 41 times.
- **(c) Non-gating diagnostics.** The committed script prints the error against time for the chaotic ensemble (median and maximum at each output time), so that the exponential growth is visible. No threshold.
- **Rule applied (Opus 5.5's condition, run before this commit).** 20 disjoint samples of 30 states, drawn by the recipe of (a) from the calibration streams `np.random.default_rng([4242, k])`, `k = 0..19` (disjoint from the gate streams `[20261010, 2000]` to `[20261010, 2003]`); RK4 at `dt = 0.01` against DOP853 at `1e-12`. The largest sample maximum over output times up to `t = 10` was **6.21e-5**, above the threshold of 3e-5, so the predeclared fallback applies: **`T = 8`, with `dt` and `τ` unchanged.** The largest sample maximum over output times up to `t = 8` was **1.14e-5** (median of the 20 maxima 3.7e-6), at most 3e-5, so the gate is frozen at `T = 8`; `τ_chaos` is 8.8 times the largest maximum observed. All 20 sample maxima, uncommitted calibration (each is the maximum over 30 states and the output times):
  - up to `t = 10`: 3.9e-6, 3.8e-6, 3.5e-6, 7.2e-6, 3.5e-5, 2.3e-5, 1.2e-5, 5.4e-6, 6.4e-6, 7.3e-6, 8.7e-6, 8.6e-6, 1.1e-5, 6.2e-5, 1.9e-5, 5.0e-6, 5.4e-5, 1.9e-5, 1.5e-5, 2.2e-5;
  - up to `t = 8`: 1.7e-6, 1.5e-6, 1.4e-6, 5.4e-6, 1.1e-5, 7.8e-6, 1.6e-6, 2.1e-6, 8.6e-7, 5.3e-6, 2.9e-6, 2.4e-6, 4.7e-6, 5.5e-6, 5.6e-6, 2.9e-6, 2.6e-6, 4.8e-6, 4.4e-6, 5.5e-6.

  The single 30-state sample that first suggested `T = 10` had a maximum of 9.8e-6, and a 6-state sample 2.3e-6: the maximum of a sample grows with its size, and the 20 maxima span a factor of 18. That is why one sample was too thin a basis for a tenfold margin.
- **Horizon rationale.** `λ` is not measured until milestone 5, so the horizon is stated in units of `√(L1/g)` and will be restated in Lyapunov times afterwards, which is a restatement and not a change. The agreement time depends only logarithmically on the tolerance, `T* ≈ (1/λ) ln(τ/ε)` (the walkthrough, D-041), so a tenfold looser or tighter `τ` moves the horizon by only `ln(10)/λ`. That is why the horizon is locked together with the tolerance.
- **What it catches, uncommitted lab on sample `[4242, 0]` at `dt = 0.01`, `T = 8`.** Classical RK4 1.68e-6 (maximum over the 30 states); W1 1.35e-2, W2 1.05e-3, W3 5.34e-1, against `τ_chaos = 1e-4`. W2's median, 8.9e-5, is below the tolerance, so it is the maximum that fires. Order defects are caught first by D-044 and D-045; this gate is the backstop for the fast integrator on chaotic motion.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** the conditional on `T` and its fallback (the proposal was `T = 10`); (b) and (c) approved unchanged. **Q9, answered:** the worst case over a frozen sample is the right statistic because it is deterministic and conservative, but its margin has to be judged against how sample maxima vary, not against one maximum, which is why the 20-sample check was added. A percentile statistic would be easier to defend and weaker at catching bugs.
- **Alternatives:** a percentile statistic; `T = 20` with `dt = 0.005` and a looser tolerance; per-state tolerances.

### D-047 Wiring check and energy anchor (P6, AMEND; follow-up CONFIRM)
- **Why:** with the default parameters all equal to one, a dropped, swapped or unused `params` in the RK4 stages or in the SciPy wrapper would hide (D-028). RK4 and the wrapper are compared with each other, so an error that both share, for example the same dropped `params`, is invisible to the comparison; hence the anchor on energy.
- **Choice:** 10 parameter sets drawn by the D-031 recipe from `np.random.default_rng([CASES_SEED, 2002])` (per attempt `rng.uniform(log 0.5, log 2, size=5)`, exponentiated, redrawn until every pair differs by at least 10 percent); 3 states per set from `np.random.default_rng([CASES_SEED, 2003])`, set by set in order (per attempt `rng.uniform(−π, π, size=2)`, `ω = 0`, kept only if `E > 0` under that set's parameters). For each set, `t_s = √(L1/g)`, `dt = 0.005 t_s`, `T = 2 t_s`, with outputs at `t = 0, t_s, 2 t_s`.
  - **Agreement:** RK4 versus the wrapper (`1e-12`) on the nondimensional state `(θ1, θ2, ω1 t_s, ω2 t_s)`; maximum error at most **1e-5**.
  - **Energy anchor (added by Opus 5.5):** for both the RK4 trajectory and the wrapper's, `max_t |E(t) − E(0)| / E_s ≤ 1e-5`, with `E` evaluated by `double_pendulum.energy` under that set's own parameters and `E_s = g((m1 + m2) L1 + m2 L2)`, the largest possible `|V|`.
- **Lab, uncommitted:** correct wiring gave an agreement maximum of 2.9e-8 and an energy drift of 1.44e-8 (RK4) and 2.8e-12 (wrapper). With `L1` and `L2` swapped in RK4 only, the agreement with the true reference was at least 0.11. With both integrators given the **same** wrong parameters, the agreement stayed at 2.3e-8 to 2.2e-7 (blind), while the energy anchor under the true parameters had these minima over the 30 cases: 7.04e-3 (`L1`, `L2` swapped), 1.46e-3 (`g` replaced by 1), 1.55e-2 (all parameters replaced by the defaults) and 5.51e-4 (`m1`, `m2` swapped). That is 55 to 1,550 times above the gate, while the correct drift is about 690 times below it.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** added the energy anchor. Its first text normalized by `|T(0)| + |V(0)|`; the implementer pointed out that this equals `|V(0)|` for `ω = 0` states and is not bounded away from zero (the smallest in the lab sample was 2.9 percent of `E_s`), so an unlucky frozen state could inflate a correct drift; the follow-up confirmed the fixed scale `E_s`. Opus 5.5 also withdrew its first estimate of the bug signal: "order 0.1" is the typical size, and the figure to quote is the minimum over the cases, 5.51e-4.

### D-048 Register and script (P7, AMEND)
- **Choice:** the thresholds, grids, stream identifiers and counts of D-044 to D-047 go into `src/horizon/tolerances.py` as documented constants, in the commit that declares them; the git history of that file is the D-025 audit trail. One committed script, `experiments/stage1/integrator_validation.py`, prints (writes no files, makes no figures) the measured slopes, errors and the non-gating growth curve of D-046(c), using the same constants. The directory `experiments/stage1/` is the one in brief §5; it does not exist yet and is created by that commit. Commit order, as in milestone 2: this decision log; the derivation note `docs/derivations/integrators.md` with its claim-to-test table; the tests plus an interface skeleton (red); the implementation (green).
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** the script prints a provenance header (git SHA, a dirty flag, and the Python, `jax`, `jaxlib`, `numpy` and `scipy` versions; D-019). Any milestone 3 number that later appears in a report must come from the milestone 7 JSON entry point, not from copied printout.
- **Why:** printed numbers without provenance break D-019 once they are quoted.

### D-049 Other items (P8, APPROVE)
- **Choice:** `scipy` becomes a direct runtime dependency (lower bound the locked `1.18.1`) in the commit that first imports it; D-014 already covers it. No parameter validation in milestone 3 (the positivity precondition is documented in `Params` in the pre-public docs cleanup). The wrap helper stays in milestone 5. No `pytest.mark.timeout` override is planned, since the expected laptop runtimes are seconds per test (D-036); any test that needs one is declared and logged first.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** nothing.

### Questions Q10 and Q11, and risks raised by Opus 5.5
- **Q10: nothing looks tuned to pass; what each check catches.** A wrong stage weight, or RK2, fails the slope tests of D-044 and D-045. A wrong stage placement that keeps the stability polynomial fails only the nonlinear exact problems. A one-step time offset gives an error of order 1e-2 against `τ_chaos = 1e-4`, and a one-row output offset an error of order one. Parameters dropped by one integrator are caught by the agreement part of D-047, and dropped by both only by its energy anchor. The agreement tolerance is deliberately loose for subtly wrong code, because order defects are the job of D-044 and D-045.
- **Q11: nothing needs the owner's decision.** For the owner's information: the agreement horizon is stated in units of `√(L1/g)`, it fell back from `T = 10` to `T = 8` under D-046's rule, and it will be restated in Lyapunov times after milestone 5.
- **The reference is only a pointwise reference over limited horizons.** In chaotic states DOP853 at `1e-12` already differs from a tighter run by about 8e-8 at `T = 20` (uncommitted, above). **Known limitation, carried into milestones 5 and 6 and into the Stage 2 data and valid-prediction-time measurements:** beyond some horizon, compare statistics, not trajectories.
- **The `1e-13` reference** is used for the convergence study (D-045) and the regular-state agreement (D-046b) only; the derivation note will say so. The wrapper's default stays `1e-12`.
- **Batching over parameters needs a `Params` of arrays.** A contract test, in the style of T11, runs `rk4_trajectory` under `jax.vmap` over a `Params` whose fields are arrays, so that the batched path is exercised.
- **Compile cost.** Each distinct `(f, n_out, stride)` recompiles. That is fine now; the runtime is watched once the CI suite runs many grids.
- **Calibration seeds stay out of the gates.** The lab used seeds 31415, 27182, 16180 and 99991 and the streams `[4242, k]` and `[4243, k]`; the gate streams are `[20261010, 2000]` to `[20261010, 2003]`.

## Milestone 3: build and results (2026-10-12)

### D-050 Milestone 3 built; every predeclared gate passed (2026-10-12)
- **Built, in commit order**, on the branch `milestone-3-integrators` (local, not pushed): `276581b` the derivation note with its claim-to-test table; `4a59355` the declared values in `src/horizon/tolerances.py` with the tripwire test K1; `318c0cf` the tests and an interface skeleton (red: of the 73 tests in the three new test files, counting K1 from the previous commit, 24 passed because they do not need the implementation, and the other 49 were red: 48 with the stub's `NotImplementedError` (45 failures and the 3 setup errors of the wiring fixture) and one, S3, with the exact-type guard described below, which reports that same stub error as the wrong type; checked by running the tests at `318c0cf`); `406ac90` the implementation, with `scipy>=1.18.1` as a direct dependency (D-049); `fbe4455` the script. The agent wrote all of it after the walkthrough (D-039, D-041).
- **The owner's rule of 2026-10-12 was followed.** The tolerances and horizons were locked as approved and were not changed after seeing results from the implementation. At `fbe4455`, `git diff 318c0cf HEAD -- tests src/horizon/tolerances.py` is empty: no test, threshold, grid, horizon or seed stream was touched after the implementation existed. All 73 tests passed on the first run against the implementation, and the full suite passes (482 tests; 43.3 s in the last local run). No decision was deferred to Opus 5.5 during the build, because no threshold, grid, horizon or interface item changed (D-025).
- **Results.** The output of `uv run python -m experiments.stage1.integrator_validation`, run from a clean working tree at commit `fbe4455` (the script's own provenance header; the laptop; exit status 0). It is the script's printout, pasted unedited, and the numbers in a report must come from the milestone 7 entry point (D-048).

- **Reading the output.** Every statistic is inside its predeclared bound. The exact-solution slopes are 4.000, 4.029, 3.993 and 3.995 (band 3.9 to 4.1); the finest-step errors are 5.3 to 9.9 percent of their ceilings; the wrapper is within 5.1e-12 of the exact solutions (bound 1e-10). The pendulum slopes run from 3.884 to 4.005 (band 3.8 to 4.2), so the tightest bound of the build is state 7's slope: 3.884 is 0.084 above the lower edge and uses 58 percent of the band's half-width, the largest fraction used by any check; state 6, at 3.889, is next with 56 percent, and every other check uses less than 30 percent of its bound (the largest of those is the linear problem's slope, 4.029, at 29 percent, which is 0.071 below the upper edge of its narrower band); the validity rule's smallest ratio is 128 against 30. The chaotic-ish worst case is 3.6e-6 against 1e-4 and the regular-state worst case 2.2e-8 against 1e-6. The wiring check gives 3.2e-8 for agreement, 6.8e-9 for RK4's energy drift and 2.2e-12 for the wrapper's, each against 1e-5. These are the sizes the uncommitted calibration of D-044 to D-047 predicted (the exact-problem errors at `dt = 0.005` are the same to the digits logged there; sections B, C and D use seeded states that the calibration did not).
- **What passing shows, and what it does not.** It shows that RK4 converges at fourth order on four problems with closed forms, two of them nonlinear; that RK4 and the DOP853 wrapper agree to the declared tolerances over the declared horizons on the seeded states; and that both integrators are consistent with their own parameters on ten non-default sets, in agreement and in energy. It does not show agreement beyond those horizons (in chaotic motion no claim is made past `T = 8`, D-046), energy behaviour over `t = 1000` or time reversibility (milestone 4), the Lyapunov estimators (milestone 5), or anything about Linux: the branch has not been pushed, so CI has not run.
- **Lab mutants, uncommitted and not results (no script is committed, as in D-038).** Six deliberate breakages of the implementation were run against the new tests, and each was caught by at least one test; the file was restored byte for byte after each. `k3` evaluated from `k1` (R1, R2, R5, G1 to G6, N3). The all-½ tableau of section 2.4 (R2, G1 to G6, N3, but not R1, which is blind to it, as the note says). `params` replaced by the all-ones parameters in RK4 only (G5, G6, N3). `params` dropped in the wrapper only (S1, S2, G5, G6, N3). One RK4 step too few per output row (R3, G1 to G5, N3). SciPy's clamp warning swallowed (S4 only).
- **Details that D-042 left open, filled in by the implementer.** They only add checks or fix a type, and none changes a number for `float64` input. `rk4_step` is not compiled by itself, since D-042 asks for compilation of `rk4_trajectory` only; its docstring says to wrap it in `jax.jit` with `f` static. `n_out` and `stride` must be `int`: a `bool` or a float raises `TypeError`, a value below 1 raises `ValueError`. `t_eval` must also be one-dimensional and finite, besides D-042's rules. `rk4_step` and `rk4_trajectory` cast the state to `float64`. `solve_reference` does not validate the shape of `state0`. Any of these is a one-line change before the merge if the owner wants it different.
- **Two observations.** `NotImplementedError` is a subclass of `RuntimeError`, so an unfinished stub passed the obvious test for D-042's `RuntimeError` on a failed solve; S3 therefore checks the exact exception type, and on the skeleton it failed for exactly that reason. And SciPy 1.18.1 reports the finite-time blow-up used in S3 through `status = -1` with no warning, so S3 rests on the status and not on a warning.
- **Cost and limits carried forward.** The three new test files take about 16 s on the laptop (15.6 s in the last run, an uncommitted timing); the slowest test is G4 at `T = 50` with 4.05 s, then G3 with 2.49 s and G2 with 2.16 s, far from the 60 s limit (D-036). CI timings are not known yet. `rk4_trajectory` still recompiles for each distinct `(f, n_out, stride)`. The strict warning filter in `solve_reference` will report, and not hide, an unrelated warning if SciPy or JAX ever emits one during the solve. The pointwise-reference limitation and the D-032 conditioning caveat stand (D-046, D-049 and the risk list above).

### D-051 Milestone 3 review, one defect fixed, Linux CI and merge (2026-10-12)
- **The owner's instructions of 2026-10-12.** Push the branch and open a draft pull request; review `src/horizon/integrators.py` and the gate tests with comments only; confirm that the gate used the pre-locked tolerance and horizon; if clean, mark the pull request ready and merge with a merge commit. After the review the owner added: fix only the `t_eval=[0.0]` case, with one test and two docstrings; do not touch the locked tolerance or horizon; once CI is green, have Opus 5.5 look at just that diff before merging.

Each equals the constant in `src/horizon/tolerances.py` (compared by an uncommitted probe). The constants entered once, in `4a59355` (2026-10-12 08:34), before the implementation `406ac90` (08:41). After the red-tests commit `318c0cf`, `tolerances.py`, the gate tests, the helpers and the register tests were not touched. D-044 to D-049 were not edited after they were pushed: the only change to the log between `0245058` and the merged head is D-050, 79 lines added and none removed. The gate tests read the values from `tolerances.py` and hold no threshold literal of their own, and K1 pins all of them.
- **Review of `integrators.py` and the gate tests.** The agent wrote them, so this is a self-review, helped by a read-only probe (uncommitted, deleted). The interface, the scheme and the claim-to-test table agree with D-042, D-043 and the note, and every gate test asserts what its docstring says. The negative controls N1 to N3 fail for the intended reasons: the wrong schemes have slopes of about 2, 2 and 1 against the band 3.9 to 4.1, swapped lengths give an agreement error of 2.94 against 1e-5, and the wrong-parameter cases pass the agreement check at 3.4e-8 or less and fail the energy anchor with drifts of 0.38 to 2.0. The six lab mutants of D-050 were run again on the tree of `5a03241`, and each is caught by the tests D-050 lists. Verifying D-050 also made the agent correct three statements it had written from memory, before the branch was pushed: the ranking of the margins (state 6's slope, 3.889, uses 56 percent of its band, not 29), the red state (48 tests failed with the stub's `NotImplementedError` and one with the exact-type guard, not 49 with `NotImplementedError`), and a timing comparison it could not reproduce, now replaced by measured figures.
- **One defect, found by the review.** `solve_reference(f, state0, params, t_eval=[0.0])` raised `AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'T'`, although `[0.0]` meets the documented rules. For a zero-length span SciPy 1.18.1 reports status 0 but returns empty lists for `t` and `y`. No gate, test or caller reached it, and no reported number was affected.
- **The fix, within the owner's scope.** `319b788` adds the test (S2: exactly one row equal to the initial state, `float64`, and no shared memory with the caller's array); it fails on the unfixed code with that `AttributeError`. `8117f39` returns a copy of the initial state when `t_eval` is `[0.0]`. The case is handled after the `solve_ivp` call and the status check, so SciPy still validates `y0` (a 2-D `y0` still raises `ValueError`) and the strict warning filter still applies (`rtol = 1e-14` still raises); both were checked by an uncommitted probe. The same commit corrects two docstrings: the module docstring said that no function takes a times array, which is wrong for `solve_reference`, and the Raises section of `solve_reference` now lists every condition on `t_eval` and says that any warning raised during the solve, from SciPy, JAX or `f`, becomes an exception. The two commits touch only `tests/test_integrators.py` (18 added lines) and `src/horizon/integrators.py`; relative to `318c0cf`, the gate tests, the helpers, the register tests and `tolerances.py` are identical.
- **Opus 5.5 reviewed only that diff, after CI was green: approve with nits, none blocking.** The fix is correct and sufficient, since validation makes `times[-1] == 0.0` reachable only for a single zero; the placement after the solve is right; the docstrings match the code; nothing locked was touched. The nits: (1) the comment should say that a single zero, `[0.0]` or `[-0.0]`, gets there; (2) the test's `dtype` assertion cannot fail for a `float64` input, so use a list or an integer array; (3) a committed test that `rtol = 1e-14` with `t_eval = [0.0]` still raises, so the warning-filter claim does not rest on an uncommitted probe; (4) add the new case to row S2 of the claim-to-test table and record the finding in this log. Nit 4 is done in the commit that adds this entry. Nits 1 to 3 are queued in `PROGRESS.md`, because each is a further code or test change outside the scope the owner set, and the pull request was merged at the head Opus 5.5 had reviewed.

## Milestone 4: decisions deferred to Opus 5.5 (2026-10-13)

The brief (section 3, Stage 1) lists five validation tests. Milestone 3 delivered two of them: convergence order (G1, G2) and agreement of RK4 and DOP853 (G3, G4). Milestone 4 adds the other three: energy conservation under the reference integrator, the small-angle FFT, and time reversibility (the plan, milestone 4). Opus 5.5 reviewed the design below on 2026-10-13 and set five conditions (C1 to C5) that had to run before any number was logged; they have run, and their results are in the entries. **No code and no test exists for milestone 4 yet.** Every number labelled "uncommitted lab" comes from a throwaway script on the merged code, with calibration states from `np.random.default_rng([4343, k])`, streams that are disjoint from the gate streams 3000 to 3006; none is a result, and no calibration script is committed. Items that belong to the owner and not to Opus 5.5 are listed at the end of this section.

### D-052 Scope and shape of milestone 4 (P1, APPROVE)
- **Choice:** new tests only for brief tests 1 to 3: **E** (energy drift), **F** (small-angle FFT) and **V** (reversibility), each with negative controls (**N**), recipe checks for the seeded cases (**C**) and a tripwire (**K**), in the style of D-044 to D-048. Convergence order and agreement are not duplicated: the script prints their numbers from the milestone 3 measurement functions, so that all five validations appear in one printout. Files, in this order of work: `docs/derivations/physics_validation.md` (the note, with its claim-to-test table and a list of what each test cannot catch); the milestone 4 section of `src/horizon/tolerances.py`; `tests/physics_checks.py` (shared definitions and measurements, used by the tests and by the script); `tests/test_physics_validation.py` (E, F, V, N); `tests/test_physics_register.py` (K, C); `experiments/stage1/physics_validation.py` (prints the five validation numbers with a provenance header and the clean-tree git SHA, writes no files). Every constant of the design (horizons, amplitudes, sampling, padding, peak separation, state counts, streams, ceilings) lives in the register and is pinned by K1; `physics_checks.py` and the script define no number of their own. No change to the integrators, the mechanics, or any milestone 2 or 3 test or tolerance. No new dependency: the FFT, the Hann window (`np.hanning`) and the parabolic interpolation are NumPy. No symplectic integrator (deferred in the plan).
- **No red phase.** The code under test already exists, so the new tests pass on their first run. The evidence that each gate can fail is the committed controls (D-058) and an uncommitted mutant lab run before the results entry, whose numbers are not committed (as in D-038 and D-050).
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** nothing in the scope; the shared module and the script are covered by the register through K1, as above. At the D-053 sign-off it added that K1 also asserts that the energy statistic takes its scale from the parameters only.
- **Alternatives:** duplicating G1 to G4 in the milestone 4 suite; a symplectic integrator now.
- **Why:** the plan asks for the three tests and for the five numbers to be recorded by a committed script; repeating milestone 3's tests would split one claim over two places.

### D-053 Energy-drift statistic and normalisation (P2; the brief's wording is logged as an error)
- **Choice:** `drift = max over the output times of |E(t) - E(0)| / E_s`, with `E_s = g ((m1 + m2) L1 + m2 L2)`, the largest possible `|V|` (D-047). **`E_s` is a fixed scale set by the parameters alone**, not by the state or the trajectory, so one number applies to every state of a parameter set. The code takes it from `energy_scale(params)` and never from a state-dependent form such as `max(|E(0)|, E_s)` or `max |E(t)|`, and K1 asserts that the statistic takes its scale from the parameters only. **The E1 ceiling stays `1e-9`, the brief's number, unchanged** (D-054).
- **The brief's wording is an error, and it is logged here as one.** Brief test 1 says "relative energy drift ... below ~1e-9". Read literally, `|dE| / |E(0)|` is undefined at `E(0) = 0`, is ill-conditioned near it, and measures the initial condition as much as the integrator: `|dE|` is almost independent of the state while `|E(0)|` can be tiny, and `|E(0)|` depends on the arbitrary zero of the potential energy (`E` is defined only up to an additive constant), so a fixed threshold on it is not a well-posed statement about the integrator. What is in error is the normaliser; the number `1e-9` is kept. At the brief's own tolerance the literal ratio exceeds the brief's own number. Uncommitted lab, DOP853 at `rtol = atol = 1e-12`, `t = 1000`, 20 rest-start chaotic-ish states (stream `[4343, 10]`) with `E(0)` in `[0.016, 2.891]`: against `E_s` the worst drift is 4.66e-10 (median 3.02e-10, smallest 2.18e-10) and none exceeds 1e-9; against `|E(0)|` the worst is 4.17e-8, about 40 times the threshold (median 9.96e-10), and 8 of the 20 exceed 1e-9. For 10 moving states (stream `[4343, 12]`) the literal ratio exceeds 1e-9 in 2 (worst 1.87e-9) and the `E_s` ratio in none (worst 7.45e-10). The brief is the owner's document and is not edited; this entry is the correction, and the reading above is the one every later document uses.
- **Transparency (the owner's condition):** the script and the report also show the literal `|dE| / |E(0)|`, non-gating, for every gate and moving state whose `|E(0)|` is not near zero, defined as `|E(0)| >= 0.1 E_s` (0.3 for the default parameters). The constant `E_LITERAL_FLOOR` is report-only, declared in the register and pinned by K1; its register comment must say that the floor test uses `E` as `double_pendulum.energy` defines it (the milestone 2 zero of potential) and `E_s` from `energy_scale`. States below the floor are counted and their `E(0)` listed, with no ratio printed, because there the ratio would measure `|E(0)|` and not the integrator; the `E_s` drift is printed for every state anyway. A displayed literal ratio can exceed `1e-9` whenever `|E(0)| < D E_s / 1e-9`, where `D` is the `E_s`-normalised drift; at the lab's worst `D = 4.66e-10` that is `|E(0)| < 1.4`. For example, a state with `E(0) = 0.63` and `D = 2.55e-10` has a literal ratio of `2.55e-10 x 3 / 0.63 = 1.2e-9` (a calculation from two lab values). The report says so wherever it happens.
- **Provenance of the change:** the normaliser was decided on the calibration streams `[4343, k]` only. No gate state (streams 3000 to 3006) had been evaluated under either metric when it was decided (D-059).
- **Owner and Opus 5.5:** Opus 5.5 approved P2 on the condition that the owner accepts the reading. **The owner accepted it on 2026-10-13, on three conditions:** `E_s` is a fixed scale set by the parameters alone and the `1e-9` threshold is unchanged; the report also shows the literal ratio where `|E(0)|` is not near zero; and, because this changes a gate metric after the literal metric failed in the lab, Opus 5.5 signs off on the final form before it is locked. **Opus 5.5 signed off on 2026-10-13, with three wording amendments** (the arbitrary zero of the potential in the error paragraph, the exact condition for the literal ratio to exceed `1e-9`, and the provenance bullet), all included above. It also accepted the 0.1 floor and the treatment below it, and found that "error" is accurate once the zero-of-potential argument is stated. None of its amendments changes the statistic, a ceiling or a gate; the F and V gates use no energy.
- **Alternatives, both rejected because they make the gate depend on which states are chosen or on the zero of the potential:** the literal ratio restricted to `|E(0)| >= 1`; normalising by `max(|E(0)|, 1)`.
- **Why:** `E_s` is the scale already used in D-047, it is defined for every state, and it makes the gate a statement about the integrator and not about the initial condition.

### D-054 E1: the DOP853 energy-drift gate (P3, APPROVE with an addition; Q1, Q2)
- **Choice:** DOP853 at its defaults (`rtol = atol = 1e-12`), `T = 1000`, outputs at every integer time, the statistic of D-053, **ceiling exactly `1e-9`** (the brief's number: it is not derived from data and is not changed). Ensemble: **2 regular rest states** (stream 3000: `rng.uniform(-0.4, 0.4, size=(2, 2))`, `omega = 0`) and **5 chaotic-ish rest states** (stream 3001: the recipe of D-046(a), `E > 0`), all at `T = 1000`. One test per state, each with **`@pytest.mark.timeout(300)`, declared here before the first run** (D-036). Uncommitted lab, laptop: 2.6 to 6 s per regular state, 9.6 to 12 s per chaotic-ish state, 11 to 18 s per moving state; the Linux runner has been 2.4 to 3.4 times slower than the laptop on this suite, so 300 s leaves at least 7 times headroom for the slowest. Expected cost: about 60 s on the laptop for the seven states plus about 13 s for the two control runs, so about 3 to 4 minutes on the runner; the CI job limit stays 20 minutes (no workflow change). **Moving states** (stream 3006: 8 states, `theta` uniform in `[-pi, pi]`, `omega` uniform in `[-2, 2]`) are measured and reported by the script and **not gated**. For every gate and moving state the script prints `max |dE| / E_s` up to `t = 100`, `300` and `1000` from the same run (the growth law of the drift), and per ensemble the maximum, median and minimum, so that "report the actual number" means a distribution and not a pass.
- **Acceptance rule for the ensemble (Opus 5.5's condition C2, run):** the largest drift among 20 disjoint calibration states must be at most `0.5 x 1e-9`. Uncommitted lab: rest-start chaotic-ish states (n = 20) largest 4.66e-10; regular states (n = 12, stream `[4343, 11]`) largest 1.58e-11, median 1.73e-12. The rule holds (4.66e-10 is at most 5e-10) and the margin to the ceiling is 2.1 times.
- **Why moving states are not gated (Q2):** uncommitted lab, moving states (n = 10) largest 7.45e-10, median 3.08e-10, and a hand-picked moving state `(0.5, -0.5, 3, -2)` gave 8.76e-10, so they come within 1.3 and 1.1 times of 1e-9. A gate with that margin would be a coin flip as the ensemble grows. The report must say that moving states come that close.
- **Does E1 discriminate the reference tolerance? (condition C3, run):** four calibration chaotic-ish states (stream `[4343, 300]`), `t = 1000`, uncommitted lab. `rtol = atol = 1e-12`: 2.96e-10, 3.13e-10, 3.44e-10, 2.29e-10 (largest 0.34 times the ceiling). `1e-11`: 2.82e-9, 3.21e-9, 3.60e-9, 2.46e-9 (largest 3.6 times the ceiling). `1e-10`: 3.24e-8, 3.94e-8, 4.16e-8, 3.19e-8 (largest 42 times the ceiling). The drift scales about linearly with the tolerance, so E1 tells `1e-12` from `1e-11`. The `1e-10` run on the first two chaotic-ish gate states is kept as a **committed negative control** (D-058).
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** the script also prints the drift growth at `t = 100`, `300`, `1000`; the mix of states (2 regular and 5 chaotic-ish, not 3 and 4, because the regular states have about 60 times margin and carry little information); `t = 1000` for every state, not a shorter horizon for some.
- **Known risk:** DOP853 drift near 5e-10 is partly accumulated rounding, which can differ between macOS and Linux. A gate state that fails only on Linux is a finding to investigate, never a reason to loosen the ceiling (D-025).
- **Alternatives:** gating the moving states; `t = 200` for more states; a larger ensemble.
- **Why:** the ceiling is the brief's number, and the chaotic-ish states are the binding case.

### D-055 E2: the RK4 energy-drift gate, and the rule for every calibrated ceiling (P4, AMEND; Q3)
- **Rule, declared now for every calibrated ceiling of milestone 4:** the ceiling is **10 times the largest of 20 disjoint calibration sample maxima**, each sample having the size of the gate ensemble and being drawn from `[4343, k]` streams, **rounded up to the next value of the form 1, 2 or 5 times a power of ten**. A ceiling set this way is not changed after the gate has run against the implementation (D-025). Two values are not set by this rule: the E1 ceiling `1e-9`, which the brief fixes, and the F tolerance `1e-5`, whose cases are deterministic (D-056).
- **Choice:** RK4 at `dt = 0.01` (`AGREE_DT`, `stride = 100`), `T = 1000`, outputs at every integer time, the statistic of D-053, on **12 regular rest states** (stream 3002) and **30 chaotic-ish rest states** (stream 3003), one test per ensemble; moving states are reported only. Uncommitted lab, 20 disjoint samples per ensemble (condition C1, run); the 20 sample maxima, in order:
  - regular (12 states per sample, streams `[4343, 100 + k]`): 1.43e-9, 1.57e-9, 3.43e-9, 5.03e-9, 1.51e-9, 1.46e-9, 2.85e-9, 3.68e-9, 2.53e-9, 4.8e-9, 4.62e-9, 5.44e-9, 2.88e-9, 5.12e-9, 1.83e-9, 1.7e-9, 2.84e-9, 1.25e-9, 3.72e-9, 2.01e-9. Largest 5.44e-9, median 2.845e-9; ten times the largest is 5.44e-8, so the **ceiling is `1e-7`** (18.4 times the largest sample maximum).
  - chaotic-ish (30 states per sample, streams `[4343, 200 + k]`): 1.85e-5, 2.02e-5, 2.14e-5, 2.07e-5, 2.08e-5, 1.7e-5, 2.06e-5, 2.07e-5, 2.25e-5, 1.38e-5, 2.04e-5, 2.45e-5, 1.78e-5, 1.78e-5, 1.66e-5, 2.24e-5, 1.77e-5, 2.23e-5, 2.13e-5, 2.62e-5. Largest 2.62e-5, median 2.065e-5; ten times the largest is 2.62e-4, so the **ceiling is `5e-4`** (19.1 times).
- RK4's drift is 4 to 5 orders of magnitude above DOP853's (chaotic-ish median 2.065e-5 against 3.02e-10), so the number is reported prominently even though the brief gates only the reference integrator. Its order is already gated by G1 and G2; E2 exists so that a gross error (a wrong stage, a wrong step scaling) shows at long horizon.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** the ceiling rule and its rounding (the proposal was "about 2e-8 and 2e-4", from one small sample).
- **Alternatives:** a gate on the reference integrator only; a 3 times margin rule, as for the chaotic gate of D-046.
- **Why:** one rule for every calibrated ceiling keeps a single, auditable way of setting numbers; the 3 times margin of milestone 3 was a margin against a ceiling that had already been chosen, not a rule for choosing one (Opus 5.5, Q3).

### D-056 F: the small-angle FFT (P5, APPROVE; no F3)
- **Choice:** brief test 2 for equal masses and lengths, `omega^2 = (g/L)(2 +- sqrt 2)`. Simulate `theta1(t)` from `A = 1e-3` with `omega = 0`, sampled at `h = 0.05 t_s` for `T_total = 1000 t_s` (20000 samples at `t = k h`, `t_s = sqrt(L1/g)`), for both integrators: DOP853 (defaults) and RK4 (`dt = 0.01 t_s`, `stride = 5`). Remove the mean, apply a Hann window (`np.hanning`), zero-pad to a power of two of at least 8 times the length, take the magnitude spectrum, take the **n largest local maxima separated by more than 40 padded bins** (the Hann main lobe is 32 padded bins; n = 1 for the single-mode states and 2 for the generic state; the analytic frequencies are not used to find the peaks), and refine each by a parabola through the log magnitudes of the peak bin and its two neighbours. Initial states: mode 1 `(A, sqrt2 A)`, mode 2 `(A, -sqrt2 A)` (the eigenvectors of `(2 +- sqrt2)`, for equal masses and lengths) and the generic `(A, 0)`. The analytic frequencies are written in the test from `2 +- sqrt 2` and `g/L`, not taken from the code under test. **Tolerance: relative error at most `1e-5`**, for every peak and both integrators. Two parameter sets: **F1** the defaults, and **F2** `m1 = m2 = 1.3`, `L1 = L2 = 0.7`, `g = 9.81` (checks the `g/L` scaling and the time unit).
- **Uncommitted lab (condition C4, run):** relative errors, `A = 1e-3`. F1 DOP853: mode 1 1.21e-7, mode 2 1.86e-6, generic 4.13e-8 and 4.58e-7; RK4: 1.21e-7, 1.86e-6, 4.14e-8 and 4.59e-7. F2: the same to three digits (the physics is the same in rescaled time), so the worst over everything is 1.86e-6 and the margin to `1e-5` is 5.4 times (Opus 5.5 required at least 3 for F2). The error at `A = 1e-3` is dominated by the physical amplitude-dependent frequency shift of mode 2 (about 1.85 `A^2`; 1.85e-4 at `A = 1e-2`, 1.66e-3 at `A = 3e-2`, in an earlier lab), not by the FFT, which is accurate to about 1e-7; that is why the amplitude is part of the register and is not changed to fit.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** the cases are deterministic, so the 20-sample rule does not apply; F2 must show at least a 3 times margin before the freeze (if it did not, it would have been reported and neither `A` nor the tolerance changed); peak finding for the generic state takes the two largest separated local maxima and not the global maximum twice; no F3, a fully general parameter set, because T8 and T9 of milestone 2 already check the general linearisation against the oracle.
- **Alternatives:** a general parameter set with the generalised eigenproblem; a shorter record; a larger amplitude with a looser tolerance.
- **Why:** the brief asks for the equal case; F2 adds the `g/L` scaling; the tolerance leaves 5 times for the shift and the estimator and is still 50 times below the effect of a 1 percent error in `m2` (D-058).

### D-057 V: time reversibility (P6, AMEND)
- **Choice:** brief test 3. Protocol: integrate forward for `T`, flip `omega`, integrate forward for `T`, flip `omega` back; the error is the largest absolute difference from the initial state over the four components. **V1** DOP853 (defaults) and **V2** RK4 (`dt = 0.01`) on two ensembles each: **12 regular rest states** (stream 3004) at `T = 100`, and **30 chaotic-ish rest states** (stream 3005) at `T = 8` (the milestone 3 horizon; in chaotic motion the amplification is exponential, so `T` is a design variable and will be restated in Lyapunov times after milestone 5). **V3**, an identity check under the IDENTITY class: for RK4, the negative-`dt` trajectory from `y` equals `S` times the forward trajectory from `S y`, `S` flipping `omega` (exact for an equivariant field, because `omega` enters the acceleration only through `omega^2`; it checks D-043's statement that a negative `dt` is legal and needed). V3 is not a second reversibility gate. The symmetry of the vector field itself is milestone 2's T6 and is not repeated.
- **Ceilings by the rule of D-055 (condition C1, run; uncommitted lab, 20 disjoint samples per ensemble of the gate size, the sample maxima in order):**
  - V1 regular, `T = 100` (streams `[4343, 100 + k]`): 2.34e-10, 3.28e-10, 2.99e-10, 2.81e-10, 2.41e-10, 2.53e-10, 2.91e-10, 2.76e-10, 3.37e-10, 2.53e-10, 2.29e-10, 3.1e-10, 2.96e-10, 2.88e-10, 2.38e-10, 2.88e-10, 2.95e-10, 2.35e-10, 3.55e-10, 4.09e-10. Largest 4.09e-10; times ten 4.09e-9; **ceiling `5e-9`** (12.2 times).
  - V1 chaotic-ish, `T = 8` (streams `[4343, 200 + k]`): 4.24e-10, 7.87e-10, 8.51e-10, 1.74e-9, 8.31e-10, 8.75e-10, 3.4e-10, 1.78e-9, 2.17e-9, 4.19e-10, 4.24e-9, 1.18e-9, 7.94e-10, 2.73e-9, 3.05e-9, 1.33e-9, 1.12e-9, 1.53e-9, 7.98e-10, 2.13e-9. Largest 4.24e-9, median 1.15e-9 (the samples spread by a factor of 12); times ten 4.24e-8; **ceiling `5e-8`** (11.8 times).
  - V2 regular, `T = 100`: 2.42e-8, 2.68e-8, 6.07e-8, 9.01e-8, 2.62e-8, 2.76e-8, 5.11e-8, 6.65e-8, 4.66e-8, 8.57e-8, 8.23e-8, 9.42e-8, 5.18e-8, 9.05e-8, 3.44e-8, 3.18e-8, 5.15e-8, 2.27e-8, 6.91e-8, 3.71e-8. Largest 9.42e-8; times ten 9.42e-7; **ceiling `1e-6`** (10.6 times).
  - V2 chaotic-ish, `T = 8`: 1.16e-5, 6.4e-6, 4.51e-5, 2.91e-5, 2.82e-5, 4.08e-5, 6.81e-6, 5.06e-5, 5.37e-5, 4.7e-6, 1.16e-5, 6.83e-5, 1.12e-5, 9.96e-5, 1.39e-4, 4.7e-5, 4.92e-5, 4.93e-5, 1.11e-5, 7.93e-5. Largest 1.39e-4, median 4.295e-5 (a factor of 30 between samples); times ten 1.39e-3; **ceiling `2e-3`** (14.4 times).
- In the same lab RK4's flip-and-integrate result and its negative-`dt` result agreed to three digits at every point, as the algebra says.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** 20 samples for both ensembles of V1 and V2, not 5 or 10 (the chaotic-ish DOP853 maxima already spread by a factor of 10 over 10 samples, too few to locate the tail), with the rule and rounding of D-055; V3 approved as an identity check.
- **Alternatives:** a 3 times margin rule; a longer chaotic horizon; reversing by negative `dt` only.
- **Why:** the brief allows a non-chaotic regime or a short horizon in a chaotic one; both are covered, and the ceilings are as far from a gross error as from the noise.

### D-058 Negative controls: can the gates fail? (P7, AMEND)
- **Choice:** every gate has a control run through the same measurement function, measured outside the `raises` block as in D-048, so that an exception from the code is an error and not a pass. Uncommitted lab on the ceilings above, showing that each fails for the intended reason:
  - **E1:** the vector field with a damping term `-gamma omega`, `gamma = 1e-6` (DOP853, `t = 1000`): regular 2.62e-5, chaotic-ish 1.67e-3, against the ceiling 1e-9. **The reference tolerance:** DOP853 at `rtol = atol = 1e-10` on the first two chaotic-ish gate states must fail E1 (the lab, on calibration states, gave 4.16e-8, 42 times the ceiling; D-054).
  - **E2:** RK4 at `dt = 0.05` on chaotic-ish states: worst 4.94e-2 against 5e-4.
  - **F:** the dynamics with `m2` scaled by 1.01 against the default analytic frequencies: relative error 5.14e-4 against 1e-5; the analytic pair `2 +- 1` instead of `2 +- sqrt2`: 2.35e-1.
  - **V1:** the damped field, regular, `T = 100`: 1.66e-4 against 5e-9.
  - **V2:** the wrong schemes W1, W2 and W3 of milestone 3 (written in `tests/test_integrator_gates.py`), on the regular ensemble at `T = 100` (ceiling 1e-6): W1 3.51e-4, W2 1.74e-5, W3 7.83e-1, all fail. On the chaotic-ish ensemble at `T = 8` (ceiling 2e-3) only W3 fails (3.16); **W1 (1.75e-3) and W2 (1.51e-3) do not**, and the same W1 gave 8.0e-4 on another sample. So the V2 controls are W1, W2 and W3 on the regular ensemble and W3 alone on the chaotic-ish one, and the note says that V2 on chaotic-ish states is a backstop for gross errors that cannot see a second-order scheme at `dt = 0.01`; order defects are the job of G1 and G2.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** added the reference-tolerance control (condition C3: had the drift not exceeded 1e-9 it would have been logged as a finding that E1 cannot tell `1e-10` from `1e-12`; it did exceed it, so the control stays). **Implementer's note:** the V2 controls are stated per ensemble, as above, because the 10 times rule put the chaotic-ish ceiling above W1's error.
- **Alternatives:** controls on the gate states only; mutating the implementation instead of the inputs.
- **Why:** a gate that cannot be shown to fail is not a gate.

### D-059 Seeds, runtime and CI (P8, P9, APPROVE)
- **Streams** (`np.random.default_rng([CASES_SEED, stream])`, `CASES_SEED = 20261010`): 3000 E1 regular (2 states), 3001 E1 chaotic-ish (5), 3002 E2 regular (12), 3003 E2 chaotic-ish (30), 3004 V regular (12), 3005 V chaotic-ish (30), 3006 moving states (8, reported only). They are disjoint from the parameter-set streams `k = 0` to `19`, from 1000 and from 2000 to 2003 (Opus 5.5). Calibration used `[4343, k]` only, and no gate state has been looked at.
- **Timeouts:** `@pytest.mark.timeout(300)` on the E1 tests (seven states and the control runs), declared in D-054; every other new test must stay under the default 60 s. Expected laptop times (uncommitted): F about 2 to 3 s per case; V about 7 s per test; E2 about 1 s. Not marked slow and not skipped (the earlier decision: measure and raise `timeout-minutes` rather than skip).
- **After the first CI run, the measured duration of the E1 tests on the runner is logged** (Opus 5.5), and the job total is watched against the 20-minute limit as milestones 5 and 6 add tests (risk: CI duration creep).
- **Alternatives:** `pytest-xdist` (a new dependency, not approved); reusing the milestone 3 streams.
- **Why:** new streams keep the milestone 3 and 4 gates independent.

### D-060 The milestone 4 gate and the step to a public repository (P10, AMEND)
- **Gate:** the committed script, run from a clean tree, prints brief tests 1 to 5 inside their predeclared bounds, **with the git SHA of the tree** in its provenance header; Linux CI is green; the results entry in this log pastes the printout unedited (D-048). Then the pre-public docs cleanup (queued in `PROGRESS.md`: the "owner's code" wording in 17 places, three docstring nits, the stale README status line, the follow-ups of D-051), and a **history and content scan using git and `rg` only (no new tool)** that must show, with its output pasted in the log: `git log --all --format='%an %ae %cn %ce' | sort -u` listing only the noreply identity, and no personal data in file contents. The visibility change itself is the owner's decision and is not deferred to Opus 5.5.
- **Scan, run now (condition C5; Opus 5.5 asked for it, and the agent ran it on the working tree and on all history):** no token, key, password-like string, phone number, local home path or sensitive file name anywhere; all of the agent's commits use the noreply identity. **But the owner's university e-mail address is present in two places:** in the text of D-021 (since the first docs commit, `80426fe`), and as the **author e-mail of the two merge commits that GitHub created when the agent merged pull requests #1 and #2 (`5373671` and `b66f6f9`)**; GitHub uses the account's primary address for the commits it creates, which defeats the intent of D-021. `docs/BRIEF.md` section 1 also contains personal background (affiliation, research and work history) that would become public. **Reported to the owner, who chose on 2026-10-13 to remove the address from D-021's text going forward: that is done, and history is unchanged.** The owner chose nothing else, so still open: the author field of the two merge commits, the identity for future merges (**no further merge is made until the owner chooses**), whether to scrub history, and the personal background in BRIEF section 1. Rewriting history is the owner's decision, and because GitHub keeps pull-request refs, removing the address from the public view would probably need a fresh repository and not a visibility change.
- **Opus 5.5 changed:** the clean-tree SHA in the printout; the history scan with the identity listing and the content scan; the finding about D-021.
- **Alternatives:** making the visibility change at the gate without a scan.
- **Why:** the author identity and the file contents become public with the history (D-012).

### Questions and risks raised by Opus 5.5
- **Q4: what changes the public surface, adds a tool, or can silently change a result?** Only D-053 (the `E_s` reading of the brief), which can change whether a result passes and therefore goes to the owner. The shared module and the script are covered by the register (D-052).
- **Reporting the drift** means the per-ensemble maximum, median and minimum and the growth law, not a pass (D-054).
- **Platform-dependent drift** is a finding to investigate and not a reason to loosen (D-054). **CI duration creep** is watched (D-059).
- **The history scan** (D-060) found the university address; that goes to the owner.

The three topics are the energy drift of the two integrators, what limits the accuracy of an FFT frequency estimate, and why the flow is reversible while a numerical method is only approximately so.

- **(d)** The personal-data findings of D-060: answered in part. The owner chose to remove the address from D-021's text going forward (history unchanged), which is done. Open: the two merge commits' author field, the identity for future merges, scrubbing history, and BRIEF section 1.

## Milestone 4: the build (2026-10-13)

### D-061 The owner's instructions after the design review, and a correction to D-056 (2026-10-13)
- **Instructions:** (1) build milestone 4 on a local branch, `milestone-4-physics-validation`, with no push and no pull request without the owner's say-so. (2) For future GitHub merges the agent passes the noreply address with `gh pr merge --author-email` and confirms on the first merge that the merge commit's author is the noreply identity; if it still shows the university address, the merge is reported to the owner. (3) The agent lists what in `docs/BRIEF.md` section 1 the owner may not want public, without editing it; the list was given in chat and the owner decides.
- **Correction to D-056:** the parenthetical "the Hann main lobe is 32 padded bins" is wrong. The padded length is the power of two that is at least 8 times the record, `2^18 = 262144` for 20000 samples, a padding factor of about 13, so the main lobe (4 original bins) is about 52 padded bins. The separation of 40 padded bins is unchanged: it only has to exclude a second local maximum on the same peak, and the two real peaks are over 2000 padded bins apart (the note, section 3.3). No value changes.
- **Not sent to Opus 5.5:** (1) and (2) are the owner's decisions about their own account and workflow, and the correction changes no value.

- **Reading the output.**
  - **E1.** All seven gate states are under `1e-9`. The chaotic-ish maximum is 4.87e-10, so the ceiling is 2.05 times it (the calibration's largest of 20 was 4.66e-10); the regular maximum is 1.49e-11. The running maximum of the drift grows roughly in proportion to `t`: for the worst state 6.33e-11 at `t = 100`, 1.65e-10 at 300 and 4.87e-10 at 1000, a factor of 7.7 for a factor of 10 in time.
  - **Moving states (not gated).** Maximum 9.52e-10, with two states at 9.31e-10 and 9.52e-10: they come within **1.05 times** of the brief's `1e-9`, closer than the 1.1 to 1.3 times logged in D-054 from the calibration, which confirms that gating them would be a coin flip.
  - **The literal ratio (D-053).** It is shown for 3 of the 5 chaotic-ish gate states (the other two have `|E(0)|` below 0.3, listed). One of the three, with `E(0) = 0.887`, has a literal ratio of 1.02e-9, above the brief's number, while its `E_s` drift is 3.0e-10: the case D-053 says can happen, and the reason the brief's literal wording could not be the gate.
  - **E2.** RK4 at `dt = 0.01`: regular maximum 2.63e-9 (ratio 0.026 of the ceiling `1e-7`), chaotic-ish 2.05e-5 (ratio 0.041 of `5e-4`). The chaotic-ish median is 7.17e-6 against DOP853's 3.00e-10, about four orders of magnitude, as logged in D-055.
  - **F.** Every peak is within 1.86e-6 relative (0.186 of the tolerance `1e-5`), for both integrators and both parameter sets; F2 reproduces F1 to three digits, as the physics is the same in rescaled time.
  - **V.** The ratios to the ceilings are 0.0755 (V1 regular), 0.0670 (V2 regular), 0.0129 (V1 chaotic-ish) and 0.0101 (V2 chaotic-ish). V3's difference is exactly 0, because the two sides are the same arithmetic with signs flipped.
  - **Tests 4 and 5** are milestone 3's numbers, unchanged from D-050 (slopes 3.884 to 4.005; chaotic-ish agreement 3.617e-6).
- **Mutants (uncommitted lab, not results; no script is committed).** Eight deliberate breakages of the code under test were run against the new tests, and each was caught by at least one test; every file was restored byte for byte, and the unmutated baseline passed (42 tests, E1 left out for speed). Failing tests by family:
  - M1, a damping term `-1e-6 omega` in the field: failing E1 7, E2 2, V1 2, V2 2, V3 2.
  - M2, `k3` evaluated from `k1` in RK4 (W1): failing E2 2, F 4, V2 2.
  - M3, DOP853 default `rtol = atol = 1e-10`: failing E1 6, V1 2 (six of the seven gate states).
  - M4, the whole field multiplied by 1.001 (a pure time rescaling): failing F 12.
  - M5, a term `+1e-6 omega2` in `alpha1` (odd in `omega`): failing E1 7, E2 1, V1 2, V2 2, V3 2.
  - M6, a wrong energy function (`m1 + m2` replaced by `m1` in `V`): failing C2 3, E1 7, E2 2.
  - M7, one RK4 step too few per output row: failing F 6.
  - M8, the FFT estimator without the parabolic refinement: failing F 12, K2 1.

  The two that only F catches, the time rescaling (M4) and the dropped step (M7), show that the blind spots of E and V stated in the note are real: a pure time rescaling conserves the energy and is time-reversal symmetric.
- **What passing shows, and what it does not.** It shows that DOP853 at the brief's tolerance conserves the energy of the declared rest-start states to `1e-9` (against `E_s`) over `t = 1000`, that RK4 at `dt = 0.01` stays within its ceilings, that the simulated small oscillations have the analytic normal-mode frequencies to `1e-5` for both integrators and two parameter sets, and that both integrators retrace their path within the ceilings. It does not show the energy conservation of moving states (reported, 1.05 times from the ceiling), reversibility beyond `T = 8` in chaotic motion, anything in a regime that the ensembles do not sample, or anything about Linux: the branch is not pushed, so CI has not run.
- **Details the design left open, filled in by the implementer.** The controls match the message "exceeds the ceiling", so a NaN cannot satisfy them. The E1 damped-field controls run on the first regular and the first chaotic-ish gate state, the reference-tolerance controls on the first two chaotic-ish gate states, and the V1 damped control on the first three regular states. The V2 controls are per ensemble, as in D-058. The FFT parameter sets live in `tests/physics_checks.py`, which also holds `FLIP`; the controls of V2 import the wrong schemes of milestone 3 from its test module. The script reuses milestone 3's report functions for tests 4 and 5 and prints the duration of each section.
- **Cost, measured on the laptop (uncommitted timing; CI has not run).** The full suite takes 199.9 s for 538 tests (483 tests took about 45 s before); the 55 new tests take about 155 s. E1: the seven gate states take 5 to 16 s each (about 72 to 76 s over two runs) and its four controls about 32 to 34 s, so about 104 s in all, against the 73 s that D-054 estimated (the estimate left out the two damped-field controls); V1 on the regular states takes about 11 s (D-059 said about 7 s). The script takes about 3.5 minutes (section 1: 165 s). With the runner 2.4 to 3.4 times slower than the laptop (milestones 2 and 3), the suite should take about 8 to 11 minutes on CI against the 20-minute job limit, a thinner margin than before; the measured duration of the E1 tests on the runner is logged after the first CI run (D-059).
- **Still to do:** push the branch and open the pull request (the owner's go-ahead); the first CI run and the E1 durations on the runner; merge with the noreply author address and confirm it (D-061); the pre-public docs cleanup and the history scan (D-060); the owner's decision on going public. The three interview questions (brief section 7) were given in chat on 2026-10-13.
