## Test tiers, xdist and compile caching: decisions deferred to Opus 5.5 (2026-10-14)

The owner asked, on 2026-10-14: "Split tests into two tiers with pytest markers. Fast tier: reduced versions (fewer seeded states, shorter horizons) that run on every push in under 2 minutes. Full tier: the existing gate tests, unchanged, which run on PRs to main. Gate tests and their tolerances must not change; only the new fast versions are smaller. Also cache compiled JAX functions in session fixtures and add pytest-xdist. Log the decision and have Opus approve the split before merging." Opus 5.5 reviewed the design below on 2026-10-14 (a read-only review; it ran nothing) and approved it with amendments to P1, P2, P3 and P5 to P9; P4 stands as written. **No code and no test exists for this work yet.** The measurements that fix the lists and sizes are in D-067, and they are uncommitted lab runs and not results. The change touches no tolerance and no gate (D-025), no code under `src/`, no existing test module, no shared helper module and no committed script (`tests/conftest.py` gains the tier hook and any fixtures, `pyproject.toml` the markers and the dependency, and the CI workflow its triggers); the fast tier is a smoke tier and is never cited as a gate. Items that belong to the owner and not to Opus 5.5 are listed at the end of this section.

### D-066 The two tiers, xdist and compile caching (P1 to P9, AMEND)
- **P1, tier model and markers (AMEND).** Choice: two markers, `fast` and `full`, and every test belongs to exactly one. A hook in `tests/conftest.py` assigns the tier at collection from a declared data module, `tests/tiers.py`; `fast` is the default and the rules name the heavy existing tests as `full`, so the gate test files stay byte-identical and are not edited to add a decorator. The new reduced tests live in new files. A guard test checks that each test has exactly one tier, that every rule matches at least one test (no stale rule) and that no gate file defines a fast test. **Opus 5.5 changed:** rules match exact node ids, or a function name plus explicit parameter ids, never broad globs; the hook is `@pytest.hookimpl(tryfirst=True)`, so the tiers exist before pytest's own `-m` deselection runs; a pytester test proves that `-m fast` and `-m full` select exactly the declared sets and that their union is the whole suite; the hook may add only these two markers, never a skip, an xfail, a timeout or a reordering, and the guard checks that too.
- **P2, what is full (AMEND).** Choice: the heavy existing gates (E1 and its controls, V1 and its controls, the DOP853 cases of F and its DOP853 controls, the milestone 3 gates G2 to G6 with N2 and N3) and, for the milestone 2 tests that are parametrized over seeded cases, a subset rule: the first K cases of each family stay `fast` and the rest are `full`, with the test code unchanged and only the marker differing. The exact list is fixed from a measured profile and logged. **Opus 5.5 changed:** the "heavy" threshold is set from runner time (about 2 s on the laptop is about 7 s on the runner at the observed 3.5 times); K for each family is a constant in `tests/tiers.py`, pinned by a tripwire test; the exact full list is logged together with the profile it came from (D-067).
- **P3, the fast versions (AMEND).** Choice: new tests in new files, with fewer seeded states and shorter horizons, for the loop-over-states gates (E1, V1, F, G2 to G6). They use the **same** tolerance and ceiling constants from `horizon.tolerances`: no new threshold anywhere, and `src/horizon/tolerances.py` is not touched. Each cites the gate it reduces and says what it cannot catch; its sizes are declared constants, pinned by tripwire tests; each has a reduced negative control (damped field, looser reference tolerance, heavier `m2`) that must still fail at the reduced size. **Opus 5.5 changed:** (1) each fast test uses the first `n` states of its gate's own stream, not a new stream, so that a fast state is a gate state and no new seed needs calibrating; (2) a reduced size is not always looser: for E and V a shorter horizon lowers the error, but for F a shorter record raises it (Opus 5.5 cited a lab value of 2.2e-6 at `T = 200` against the ceiling 1e-5; it has not been re-measured here), so each fast test's measured value at the reduced size must sit at least 3 times below the reused ceiling, on a calibration sample (uncommitted, logged in D-067). If it does not, the size changes, never the ceiling.
- **P4, validation of the reduction (APPROVE).** Choice: the eight mutants of the milestone 4 lab and the six of milestone 3 are run against the fast tier. Every mutant that the full tier catches must be caught by the fast tier, or be logged as a known limit with its reason. **Opus 5.5 changed:** each mutant runs against both tiers in the same lab session, so that the comparison is like for like; Opus 5.5 expects the DOP853-at-`1e-10` mutant to be a known limit at short horizons. The outcomes are uncommitted lab results and are labelled so.
- **P5, CI semantics (AMEND).** Choice: a push runs the fast job (`pytest -m fast`); a pull request to `main` runs the full job (`pytest`, with no `-m`, so everything runs); the job names say what they are, "fast tier (smoke)" and "full tier (gates)"; lint and format run in both; the job limit stays 20 minutes for the full job and about 10 for the fast one. **Opus 5.5 changed:** a push to `main` also runs the full tier, because merges are made locally and pushed straight to `main` (D-064), and the merged result would otherwise never get a full run (**this extends the owner's text and is flagged to the owner below**); the fast job is skipped on `main` (`if: github.ref != 'refs/heads/main'`), so that `main` does not run twice; a push to a branch with an open pull request triggers both runs, in different concurrency groups, which is intended; `workflow_dispatch` is added, so that the full tier can be run on any branch on demand.
- **P6, xdist (AMEND).** Choice: `pytest-xdist` is a dev dependency, added in the commit that first uses it (D-014), and the lock changes only by `pytest-xdist` and `execnet`; it is used in CI only and not in `addopts`, so that a plain local `pytest` stays serial and the timeout semantics of D-036 are unchanged. **Opus 5.5 changed:** the distribution mode for the full job is `worksteal` (or `load`), measured against `loadfile`, and not `loadscope` or `loadfile` by assumption, because those keep a whole module on one worker and all of E1 (about 104 s on the laptop) would stay serial, while rebuilding module-scoped fixtures (the oracle, the wiring fixture) per worker is cheap next to an unbalanced E1 worker; thread counts and XLA flags are set so that the workers do not oversubscribe the runner's two vCPUs; per-test durations are logged under xdist.
- **P7, compiled-function caching (AMEND).** Choice: session-scoped fixtures in `tests/conftest.py` only where measurement shows recompilation, and JAX's persistent compilation cache only if measurement says it pays. **Opus 5.5 changed:** the compile share is measured first (`jax_log_compiles`, `jax_explain_cache_misses`); a session warm-up saves nothing by itself, since each process compiles a jitted function once either way and the warm-up only moves that cost into fixture setup, and it pays only if tests re-create jitted closures and so recompile; fixing that inside `tests/physics_checks.py` or `src/` would be a code change to gate machinery and is out of scope here; a persistent cache, if used, lives in a per-run temporary directory, is never committed, and needs `jax_persistent_cache_min_compile_time_secs = 0` or the small functions are not cached; a part that gains less than 10 percent of the fast tier's time is dropped and logged as not worth it; results must be bitwise unchanged on a probe set, also under xdist and after any thread-flag change.
- **P8, budget (AMEND).** Choice: the fast tier takes at most 30 s serial on the laptop, which is about 105 s at the slowest observed runner pace (3.5 times); it is verified on CI after the first run. **Opus 5.5 changed:** the owner's "under 2 minutes" is measured as the fast job's test step at the worst observed runner pace, and the whole job time is reported as well (installation and setup cost about 20 to 40 s, so 30 s serial is at the edge); the aim is 25 s serial on the laptop; if a slow runner day exceeds two minutes, the fact is reported and nothing is loosened.
- **P9, process (AMEND).** Choice: this entry before any implementation; parallel workers in isolated worktrees, integrated by the coordinator; the implementation notes (lists, sizes, measurements) in the log before the code (D-067); Opus 5.5 approves the final split with an explicit approval line, as for the milestone 4 pull request; the merge needs the owner's go-ahead and is a local merge with the noreply identity (D-064). **Opus 5.5 changed:** the files are not all disjoint, because `tests/conftest.py` gets both the P1 hook and the P7 fixtures, and `pyproject.toml` and the lock are shared; each shared file is owned by one worker, and the coordinator alone owns the lock and the workflow file.
- **Answers to the questions put to Opus 5.5.** (Q1) Default `fast` is the right polarity, because a forgotten test then costs time and not coverage; the expensive case is caught by a guard on the fast job's `--durations` output (any fast test above a declared duration ceiling fails the job), enforced in the workflow and not inside a test. (Q2) The fast tests reuse the same constants and add no threshold; scaled ceilings would be new D-025 thresholds needing calibration, for no gate value. (Q3) Marking the first K parametrized cases `fast` and the rest `full` is an acceptable reduced version of unchanged tests, on two conditions: the cases are chosen by explicit parameter id, and a guard counts that each family has exactly K fast cases. (Q4) A push to `main` runs the full tier, as in P5. (Q5) xdist in CI only; signal-method timeouts work in workers because tests run in the worker's main thread, and one pytester case run under `-n 2` proves it; fixture setup still counts toward the timeout (`timeout_func_only = false`), and under parallel load every test slows down (V1 on regular states already takes 38 s of its 120 s on the runner), so the worst fraction of each timeout under xdist is logged and reported above 50 percent; xdist itself fails if workers collect different tests, which checks that the seeds are deterministic; pytester tests start a fresh pytest that does not inherit `-n`, and `test_timeout_config.py` is checked to behave the same either way. (Q6) Session fixtures only where measurement shows recompilation; the persistent cache is optional, per run, and dropped under 10 percent; its key includes the jax and XLA versions and flags, so results should be bitwise identical, and the probe set checks it; the committed scripts do not load `conftest.py`, so their committed results cannot be affected, which is verified by checking that nothing in `experiments/` imports a tests fixture. (Q7) The D-007 categories (c), a dependency (`pytest-xdist` and its dependency `execnet`), and (d), the CI semantics, apply, and so does "can silently change numerical results" (XLA threading and the compile cache), which the bitwise probe guards. Nothing here changes a predeclared tolerance or gate threshold (D-025), provided `tolerances.py` and the gate files stay byte-identical and the fast tier is never cited as a gate. (Q8) The risks and the checklist follow.
- **Supersedes, with the owner's instruction of 2026-10-14 as the reason:** the "Not added: `pytest-xdist`" of D-014 and the "a new dependency, not approved" of D-059; the "CI still runs everything" of the `slow` marker (D-018) and the "raise `timeout-minutes` rather than skipping it as `slow`" of D-020, for pushes outside `main` only (nothing is skipped on pull requests or on `main`); and the trigger of D-016 (push to `main` and all pull requests). The unused `slow` marker is removed or redefined (decided in D-067). D-036 (timeouts) is unchanged.
- **Conditions before D-067 is frozen** (Opus 5.5): (1) a serial profile of all 539 tests on the laptop, plus the latest CI durations, sets the full list and K; (2) for each fast test, its reduced value and its margin under the reused constant, at least 3 times; (3) the xdist distribution-mode comparison on CI: wall time, worst fraction of each timeout, and identical pass and fail sets against a serial run; (4) the compile-share measurement, with the warm-up or cache decision applied under the 10 percent rule; (5) the pytester proof of the hook ordering and of `-m` selection.
- **Risks raised by Opus 5.5:** the hook runs after deselection, so that `-m fast` silently selects the wrong set (covered by the pytester proof); fast-tier creep, as new tests default to `fast` and slowly push the job past two minutes; the fast tier quietly treated as "the tests" and a green fast run read as a gate (the job is named "fast tier (smoke)"); XLA threading under xdist slowing tests or changing results; stale node ids in `tests/tiers.py` after renames (the stale-rule guard covers this); conflicts in the lock file across worktrees; the runner's speed varying 3.5 times between runs, which puts the two-minute target at risk even when the serial time is unchanged.
- **Checklist for the final approval** (Opus 5.5): (1) `git diff <merge-base> HEAD` is empty for `src/horizon/tolerances.py`, every existing test file, `tests/physics_checks.py`, `src/` and `experiments/`; (2) the node ids from `pytest --collect-only -q` match: the 539 old ones are identical and the additions are exactly the new fast files; (3) every collected test has exactly one tier, `-m full` matches the logged list, and fast plus full equals the whole suite; (4) each new fast test cites its gate, uses only `horizon.tolerances` constants, takes its states from the gate's stream, and declares its sizes pinned by a tripwire test, and each reduced control fails at the reduced size; (5) the mutant table covers both tiers and every miss is logged as a known limit; (6) the lock diff contains only `pytest-xdist` and `execnet`, added in the commit that first uses them; (7) the workflow diff: pushes outside `main` run fast, pull requests to `main` and pushes to `main` run full, there is no `-m` on the full jobs, and the full job limit is 20 minutes; (8) CI is green on the final head for both jobs, with logged times: the fast test step and job, the full job under xdist, and the worst fraction of each timeout; (9) the bitwise probe matches across serial, xdist and the cache; (10) D-067 was committed before the code, and the supersession of the `slow` marker is logged; (11) the hook added no skip, xfail or timeout.
- **Alternatives:** one suite with a higher job limit (the earlier decision); decorators on the gate tests (rejected: it edits the gate files); scaled ceilings for the fast tests (new D-025 thresholds); `loadscope` or `loadfile` by default (E1 would stay serial); a compile cache kept between CI runs (Opus 5.5: per run only).
- **Why:** the full suite takes 3 to 10 minutes on the runner depending on its speed, so a smoke tier that answers on every push in under two minutes shortens the feedback loop, while the unchanged gates still run on every pull request and on `main`; reusing the constants means that the split changes no claim about the integrators.

### D-066 addendum: the owner scales the work down (2026-10-14, after the review)
- **The owner's instruction (verbatim):** "Scale the tier work down: once the measurement reports, one agent implements the split in a single pass. No parallel workers, and skip the worksteal and XLA-flag experiments unless the plain split misses the 2-minute target. Keep Opus's conditions and the gate-test guarantees; trim the checklist to the items that protect those."
- **What changes in D-066.** (P9) One agent implements the split in one pass, on this branch, with no parallel workers and no sub-agents of its own; the file-ownership rule of P9 existed only to keep parallel workers apart and no longer applies. The coordinator still writes the implementation notes (D-067) before the code. (P6) The plain split uses xdist in CI with its default distribution (`-n auto`, `--dist load`, which spreads individual tests, E1's among them, over the workers), and a tier uses it only if the laptop's `-n 2` run of that tier is at least 20 percent faster than its serial run (otherwise it runs serially; the choice is logged). The `worksteal` comparison and the XLA and thread-flag settings are **not** run, unless the fast job's test step, measured on CI, is over two minutes; then they are the first remedies tried, in that order, with the result logged. A CI measurement needs a pushed branch, which needs the owner's go-ahead (item (b) below). (P7) The compile-share measurement stays, as condition 4 of Opus 5.5; a persistent compilation cache is not tried unless that measurement and the 10 percent rule call for it, and then it carries the bitwise probe of Q6.
- **What does not change:** Opus 5.5's verdicts on P1 to P5 and P8, and its five conditions: the serial profile and the full list; the margin of at least 3 times of every fast test under the reused constant; on CI, the identical pass and fail set against serial, the wall times and the worst fraction of each timeout (only the comparison of distribution modes inside this condition becomes conditional, as above); the compile-share decision under the 10 percent rule; and the pytester proof of the hook order and of `-m` selection. Also unchanged are the guarantees for the gates: the same constants and no new threshold, the first states of each gate's own stream, and the byte-identical gate files and `tolerances.py`.
- **The checklist, trimmed by the owner to the items that protect the conditions and the gate-test guarantees** (Opus 5.5's list above is kept as it was given). (1) `git diff <merge-base> HEAD` is empty for `src/horizon/tolerances.py`, every existing test module and helper under `tests/` other than `tests/conftest.py`, `src/` and `experiments/`. (2) `pytest --collect-only -q` lists the 539 old node ids unchanged, and the additions are exactly the new fast files. (3) Every collected test has exactly one tier, `-m full` equals the logged full list, fast and full together are the whole suite, and the hook adds no skip, xfail, timeout or reordering. (4) Each new fast test uses only constants of `horizon.tolerances` and the first states of its gate's own stream, its measured value is at least 3 times below the reused ceiling, and its reduced control fails at the reduced size. (5) The workflow: pushes outside `main` run the fast tier, pull requests to `main` and pushes to `main` run the full tier with no `-m`, and the full job limit is 20 minutes. (6) The lock changes only by `pytest-xdist` and `execnet`, added in the commit that first uses them. (7) CI is green on the final head for both jobs, the full job's pass and fail set equals the serial one, and the fast test step and job times and the worst fraction of each timeout are logged. (8) D-067, including the decision on the `slow` marker, was committed before the code.
- **Dropped by the scale-down, and what that costs.** The mutant run against both tiers (P4; item 5 of Opus 5.5's checklist): the fast tier's ability to fail then rests on its reduced negative controls alone and not on a lab against the milestone 3 and 4 mutants, and a defect that only the long horizons show would be caught by the full tier alone (a known limit, which P4 already expected for the DOP853-at-`1e-10` mutant). The bitwise probe across serial, xdist and the cache (item 9): it is needed only if a persistent cache or a thread flag is adopted, and neither is unless the escalation above happens. The citation of the gate in each fast test and the pinning of its sizes by a tripwire are still done, as part of the implementation, but they are no longer approval items. Opus 5.5 gets this trimmed list and the list of what was dropped with the request for the final approval; if it finds that a dropped item is needed for the guarantees, that goes to the owner, who decided the trimming.

### Items for the owner, not for Opus 5.5
- **(a)** The full run on every push to `main` extends the owner's text, which said "PRs to main". Opus 5.5 recommends it, because merges are pushed straight to `main`. `workflow_dispatch` is a second small addition.
- **(b)** Pushing the branch and opening a draft pull request is needed to measure the fast tier, and the full tier under xdist, on the real runner. The merge needs the owner's go-ahead, after Opus 5.5's explicit approval of the final split.

## Test tiers: implementation notes (2026-10-14)

The measurements that D-066 asked for are in. They come from a lab worker that worked in a standalone clone of `717a104` outside this repository (its tests are those of `main`), and the owner has since scaled the work down (the D-066 addendum). **No code and no test exists for this work yet; this entry is committed before any.** Everything numerical below is an **uncommitted lab measurement** (the raw outputs and scratch scripts are not committed, as in D-038), taken on an Apple M3 laptop (8 logical cores, 4 performance and 4 efficiency, 16 GB) with Python 3.12.11, jax and jaxlib 0.11.2, numpy 2.5.3, scipy 1.18.1, pytest 9.1.1, pytest-timeout 2.4.0 and pytest-xdist 3.8.0 (run ephemerally with `uv run --locked --with pytest-xdist`; `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` were not touched). The 1-minute load average was 3 to 7 during the session, with two external spikes to 38 and 43. Serial runs reproduced the 190 to 200 s of earlier runs; parallel runs varied by 20 percent or more between identical runs, so parallel figures are ranges. A first `-n 2` run had 5 failures that came from a bug in the lab's own counting wrapper (it dropped keyword arguments), not from the code; it was fixed and the run repeated.

### D-067 What was measured, the full list, the sizes and the other choices (implementation notes to D-066)
- **Profile (serial, `--durations=0`).** 539 passed in 191.99 s (a second run: 190.41 s). Of the 182.77 s listed (pytest hides phases under 5 ms), `tests/test_physics_validation.py` has 137.65 s (75.3 percent), `tests/test_double_pendulum.py` 22.59 s, `tests/test_integrator_gates.py` 14.80 s, `tests/test_oracle_double_pendulum.py` 3.51 s, `tests/test_timeout_config.py` 2.23 s, `tests/test_integrators.py` 1.26 s, and the other files under 1 s each. By class: 367 tests under 0.1 s (6.03 s), 147 from 0.1 to 1 s (26.86 s), none from 1 to 2 s, and **25 of 2 s or more (149.88 s, 82.0 percent)**; the next slower tests take 0.97, 0.93, 0.92 and 0.58 s.
- **The milestone 2 tests.** The oracle is built once per process, in 1.70 s (`functools.cache`); it is the 2.05 s of `test_t1_mass_matrix_matches_the_oracle[set-00]`, a cost of whichever oracle test runs first and not of that test. The 20 moderate parameter sets have the ids `set-00` to `set-19` and 25 states each, looped inside one test; 16 functions of `tests/test_double_pendulum.py` and `test_o2_*` of the oracle file are parametrized over them (17 functions, 340 tests). One set costs 0.89 to 0.96 s over all its tests, and `set-00` costs 3.80 s with the oracle build and the first compilations.
- **JAX compilation.** XLA compilation is 6.49 s over 282 compiles, plus 0.77 s of lowering and 0.62 s of tracing: about 7.9 s, or 4 percent of a 190.41 s run. Each distinct `(f, n_out, stride)` of `rk4_trajectory` compiles once per process (180 calls with `n_out = 2, stride = 200` compile once), and `solve_reference` reuses the executable for the same `f` (the second and third calls took 0.024 s against 0.227 s for the first, with no compile; a new lambda each time recompiled). The 52 compiles of `jit(trajectory)` (2.49 s) belong to the wrong-scheme controls, which build a fresh function in each test and so cannot be shared. **There is nothing for a session fixture to cache:** a warm-up of 0.24 s gave 186.03 s against 190.41 s, which is within noise. JAX's persistent cache (both thresholds at 0; 282 entries, 2.1 MB) gave 190.29 s cold and 183.28 s warm, with XLA compile time down from 6.01 s to 0.57 s (one pair of runs, so uncertain at the 1 to 2 s level); a probe of 21 arrays (1,868 values from RK4 and DOP853 runs at the default and F2 parameters) was bitwise identical between repeats, in the cold and the warm cache, and with the warm-up. What dominates the heavy tests is the Python loop around DOP853, which is dispatch and not compilation: E1 `[chaotic-3]` makes 393,533 right-hand-side calls at 31.7 µs each, and a bare jitted call costs 25.1 µs.
- **xdist** (whole suite; 539 passed in every run; wall times against 191.99 s serial). `-n 2`: `load` 131.55 and 132.49 s (1.46 and 1.45 times); `loadscope` 136.75 s (taken just after a load spike) and 160.35 s; `loadfile` 135.96 s; `worksteal` 139.71 s (under noise: its per-test durations were inflated 1.49 times; uncertain). `-n 4`: `load` 85.53 s (2.24 times), `loadscope` 141.46 s, `loadfile` 138.47 s, `worksteal` 89.00 s. `-n auto` (8 workers): `load` 69.29 s (2.77 times), `loadscope` 140.23 s, `loadfile` 139.55 s. `loadscope` and `loadfile` put all of `tests/test_physics_validation.py` (137.7 s of work) on one worker, so their wall time is that file's whatever the worker count; under `load` at `-n 2` the workers got 62.3 s and 120.5 s of work, because the heavy tests come last in the collection. Scaling beyond four workers is poor on this machine (copies of one DOP853 job run at once took 3.8, 4.0, 4.9 and 7.4 s for 1, 2, 4 and 8 copies, and single-thread XLA flags changed nothing). Startup on a trivial selection costs 0.3 to 0.5 s. Checks: the pass sets equal serial; a digest of the seeded cases (moderate, large-angle, E1, V1, G regular and chaotic, wiring) was identical in every process of all 11 runs, the controller included; the signal-method timeout works inside workers (a busy loop and a `sleep` failed with "Timeout (>2.0s)", and a 3 s fixture failed at setup, so setup counts; the session continued); the oracle is built once per worker under `load` and `worksteal` (2, 4 and 8 builds for 2, 4 and 8 workers) and once or twice under `loadscope` and `loadfile`; the `wiring` fixture was computed once in every run (its cost would be about 0.6 s if split).
- **The tests of 2 s or more, and the tiers they leave.** Excluding exactly the 25 leaves 514 tests at 40.15 and 44.16 s serial; excluding the 15 of 3 s or more leaves 67.30 s. Excluding the 25 and keeping only the first 3 parameter sets of the milestone 2 families (289 fewer tests) leaves 23.51 and 23.61 s (K = 5 also gave 23.61 s). Once the heavy tests are gone, `tests/test_double_pendulum.py` is 24.54 of the 38.16 s listed.
- **Reduced versions, feasibility** (same constants; the first states of each gate's own ensemble; warm; load 5 to 7). *E1* (ceiling `E_DOP_CEILING = 1e-9`; drift and margin on regular-0, chaotic-0, chaotic-1): at `T = 100`, 1.66e-12 (602 times), 2.50e-11 (40.0), 2.24e-11 (44.6), 0.80 to 1.06 s each; at `T = 50`, 8.07e-13, 9.73e-12, 9.93e-12 (1,240, 103, 101 times); at `T = 200`, 3.13e-12, 5.83e-11, 5.47e-11 (320, 17.2, 18.3 times); at `T = 1000` for reference, 1.49e-11, 2.47e-10, 3.00e-10 (67, 4.05, 3.33 times). The controls still exceed the ceiling: the damped field by 4,975 and 95,269 times at `T = 100`; the looser reference tolerance (`rtol = 1e-10`, chaotic-0 and chaotic-1) by 1.5 and 2.5 times at `T = 50`, **3.5 and 4.4 times at `T = 100`**, 5.6 and 7.3 times at `T = 200`, and 30.5 and 39.2 times at `T = 1000`. *V1* (ceilings `V1_CEILING_REGULAR = 5e-9`, `V1_CEILING_CHAOTIC = 5e-8`): regular, 12 states at `T = 100` (the gate) 3.78e-10 (13.2 times, 10.8 s); 3 states at `T = 100` 6.03e-12 (829 times, 2.0 s); 3 states at `T = 20` 1.07e-12 (4,670 times, 0.44 s); chaotic-ish, 30 states at `T = 8` (the gate) 6.45e-10 (77.6 times, 4.9 s); 6 states at `T = 8` 6.21e-11 (805 times, 0.98 s); the damped control on the first 3 regular states exceeds the ceiling by 963 times at `T = 20` and 4,680 times at `T = 100`. The worst full-ensemble regular states are 5 to 7 (2.3e-10 to 3.8e-10), so the first three are about 60 times below the gate's worst and the reduced gate is correspondingly less sensitive. *F* (`F_TOL = 1e-5`; worst relative error over 12 cases: 2 parameter sets, 3 modes, 2 integrators): 4,000 samples (200 time units) 2.165e-6 (4.62 times, 2.29 s); 8,000 samples 2.025e-6 (4.94 times, 4.93 s); the gate's 20,000 samples 1.863e-6 (5.37 times, 17.46 s); at 4,000 samples the control with `m2` times 1.01 gives 5.14e-4 (51 times) and the wrong pair `2 ± 1` gives 0.235 (23,463 times). *G2* (slope band 3.8 to 4.2): the 12 states of the gate 3.8836 to 4.0052, 3.13 s; the first 3 states 3.9567 to 3.9953, 0.87 s, validity ratio at least 18.98 (the gate's smallest is 4.28; the floor is 1). *G3* (ceiling `CHAOTIC_TOL = 1e-4`): 30 states at `T = 8` 3.617e-6 (27.65 times, 2.58 s); the first 6 states give the same value (the worst state is among them), 0.86 s. *G4* (ceiling `REGULAR_AGREE_TOL = 1e-6`): 12 states at `T = 50` 2.218e-8 (45.1 times, 4.43 s); 3 states at `T = 20` 9.52e-9 (105 times, 0.61 s). *G5, N2 and N3* take 0.58, 0.52 and about 0.5 s as they are, so they are not reduced.
- **Two prototypes of the whole fast tier** (a scratch file of 27 tests outside the repository, plus the existing tests that remain; `-k` and deselect lists). *A*: E1 at `T = 100`, K = 3, keeping `o3` and the timeout test: 246 tests, 34.35 and 35.08 s serial, 21.97 s with `-n 2 load` (calm). *B*: E1 at `T = 50`, K = 3, `o3` and the timeout test moved to full: 244 tests, 31.12 and 26.63 s serial, 23.37 s (load 17) and 18.74 s (calm) with `-n 2 load`. The 27 scratch tests cost 12.8 to 13.4 s of those totals. One `-n 2` run at load 43 took 106 s and is invalid. **No CI time has been measured**, and the factor 3.5 of D-066 P8 is an earlier observation, not a measurement of this tier.
- **The full list, fixed from the profile above (D-066 P2).** The 2 s threshold (about 7 s on the runner at 3.5 times) decides which families are heavy: E1, V1, F and G2 to G4 each contain tests of 2 s or more, and so do `o3` and the timeout test. **A gate family that gets a reduced fast version is full in its entirety**, so that the reduced test replaces its gate in the fast tier and the tier does not run one property twice at two sizes; this moves 9 tests of under 2 s (the F cases of 0.04 to 1 s) beyond the threshold and keeps the fast tier near 29 s instead of near 32 s (the prototypes above, without the F tests, plus those nine). The rules name each function by its exact node id, with the number of collected cases pinned (so a renamed or added case fails the guard); `tests/test_physics_validation.py` has `test_e1_dop853_energy_drift_stays_under_the_briefs_ceiling` (7 cases: `regular-0`, `regular-1`, `chaotic-0` to `chaotic-4`), `test_e1_control_a_damped_field_fails_the_ceiling` (2), `test_e1_control_a_looser_reference_tolerance_fails_the_ceiling` (2), `test_v1_dop853_round_trip_returns_to_the_initial_state_on_regular_states` (1), `test_v1_dop853_round_trip_returns_to_the_initial_state_on_chaotic_ish_states` (1), `test_v1_control_a_damped_field_fails_the_ceiling` (1), `test_f_fft_peaks_match_the_analytic_normal_mode_frequencies` (12), `test_f_control_the_wrong_analytic_pair_fails_the_tolerance` (1) and `test_f_control_a_heavier_second_mass_fails_the_tolerance` (1), 28 tests; `tests/test_integrator_gates.py` has `test_g2_rk4_converges_at_order_four_on_regular_pendulum_states`, `test_g3_rk4_and_dop853_agree_on_chaotic_ish_states_over_the_declared_horizon` and `test_g4_rk4_and_dop853_agree_on_regular_states_over_the_declared_horizon` (3); then `tests/test_oracle_double_pendulum.py::test_o3_the_derivation_note_equals_the_oracle_symbolically` (1) and `tests/test_timeout_config.py::test_a_hung_test_fails_at_the_limit_and_the_session_continues` (1). **The subset rule, K = 3:** the ids `set-03` to `set-19` of the 16 per-set functions of `tests/test_double_pendulum.py` and of `test_o2_*` in the oracle file (17 functions times 17 sets, 289 tests); `set-00` to `set-02`, the 5 large-angle cases and the singletons stay fast, and `test_t1_mass_matrix_matches_the_oracle[set-00]`, whose 2.05 s is the oracle build, is not listed. In all, **322 existing tests are full and 217 are fast**; the full tier runs all 539 and the new fast tests. Not full, because they are cheap and are not reduced: G1, G5, G6, N1 to N3, E2, V2 and V3, the register tests and the rest.
- **The sizes of the new fast tests** (declared constants at the top of the new files, pinned by tripwire tests; each takes the first `n` states of its gate's own stream and the constants of `horizon.tolerances` unchanged). E1 at `T = 100` on the first regular state and the first two chaotic-ish states, with the damped-field control on the first regular and the first chaotic-ish state and the looser-tolerance control on the first two chaotic-ish states, as in the gate; `T = 100` is the shortest of the horizons measured (50, 100, 200) at which the looser-tolerance control exceeds the ceiling by 3 times or more. V1 on the first 3 regular states at `T = 20` and the first 6 chaotic-ish states at `T = 8`, with the damped control on the first 3 regular states at `T = 20`. F with 4,000 samples, for both parameter sets, all modes and both integrators, with its two controls. G2 on the first 3 states, G3 on the first 6 states at `T = 8`, G4 on the first 3 states at `T = 20`. The new tests are in `tests/test_fast_physics.py` and `tests/test_fast_integrators.py`; each cites the gate it reduces and says what it cannot catch. **The expected cost is an estimate from the two prototypes and not a measurement of the final tier:** about 13 s for the new tests and about 16 s for the 217 existing fast tests, so about 29 s serial on the laptop, against the budget of 30 s (P8) and the aim of 25 s. **If a run is over 30 s, the implementer turns these knobs in this order, each logged, and only while the margins below hold:** K from 3 to 2 (about 0.9 s); the second chaotic-ish E1 state; the V1 regular states from 3 to 2; the G2 states from 3 to 2; the F record from 4,000 to 3,000 samples. E1 is not shortened below `T = 100`. If the tier is still over 30 s, the implementer stops and reports.
- **The margins every fast test must show** (D-066 P3 and condition 2, made exact here; the numbers come from a calibration run that is uncommitted and logged). For a gate with a ceiling: the measured statistic is at most one third of the ceiling. For a band (the slope of G2): the worst slope lies within one third of the band's half-width of its centre (between 3.933 and 4.067), and the validity ratio is at least 3. **For each reduced control, which Opus 5.5 asked only to fail, this entry asks that its statistic exceeds the ceiling by at least 3 times**, so that a control cannot pass by a hair on another platform. That is stricter than D-066 and is for Opus 5.5 to confirm at the final approval.
- **No session warm-up fixture and no persistent compilation cache.** The measurement answers the owner's request to cache compiled functions in session fixtures: within a process each function compiles once per key, so there is nothing to cache, and the warm-up gained nothing measurable (D-066 P7: fixtures only where measurement shows recompilation). The persistent cache would have saved about 7 s of 190 s (4 percent) on the whole suite; the fast tier's share was not measured (the per-file compile times suggest something like 4 to 5 s of 29 s, which is an estimate), it would need a CI cache action and a key, and under the owner's scale-down it is not tried: it is the third remedy of the escalation, after `worksteal` and the XLA and thread flags. If the owner wants it anyway, it is a small addition (item (c) below).
- **xdist.** A tier runs under `-n auto` (two workers on the runner, with the default `--dist load`) only if the laptop's `-n 2` run of that tier, the median of three on a calm machine (load under 4), is at least 20 percent faster than its serial run, and the choice is logged. The prototypes and the full suite pass that test on the figures above (serial 26.6 to 35.1 s against 18.7 to 22.0 s for the fast tier; 192 s against 132 s for the full tier). `loadscope` and `loadfile` are not used: they would keep E1 on one worker.
- **The duration guard (D-066 Q1).** The fast job runs pytest with `--durations=0 --durations-min=15` and a following step fails the job if any test, setup or teardown of 15 s or more is listed. 15 s on the runner is about 4.3 s on the laptop at 3.5 times: above the first oracle test (2.05 s on the laptop, about 7 s at 3.5 times) and the slowest planned fast test (about 1 s), so it does not fire on a slow day, and it catches a forgotten heavy test of 4.3 s or more. The ceiling and its reason are written in the workflow. The guard catches creep; it is not a gate.
- **The `slow` marker** (D-018) is removed from `pyproject.toml`: it was never used, and its text, "CI still runs them", would mislead once pushes skip tests. `fast` and `full` are registered in its place, with `--strict-markers` unchanged.
- **Files.** New: `tests/tiers.py` (data and the pure function that assigns a tier), `tests/test_tiers.py` (the guards and the pytester proofs of D-066), `tests/test_fast_integrators.py`, `tests/test_fast_physics.py`. Changed: `tests/conftest.py` (the hook), `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` (markers; `pytest-xdist` in the commit that first uses it), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `README.md`, `PROGRESS.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md` (the results entry). Nothing else.
- **Not decided here.** Whether the fast job meets two minutes on the runner is not known and cannot be measured without a pushed branch (item (b) of D-066). If its test step is over two minutes, the escalation of the D-066 addendum applies. The mutant run and the bitwise probe are dropped (D-066 addendum).

### Item for the owner, not for Opus 5.5
- **(c)** The request to cache compiled JAX functions in session fixtures was measured and is not adopted, for the reason above. Say so if a persistent compilation cache is wanted anyway.

## Test tiers: results of the implementation (2026-10-14)

The tier work of D-066 and D-067 is implemented on the local branch `test-tiers`, by one agent in one pass, and **not pushed**. The D-067 commit precedes every code commit (`git log --oneline main..HEAD` lists the three documentation commits first). Every number below that comes from a run of the committed tests is a run on the laptop of D-067 (Apple M3, Python 3.12.11, the locked stack plus `pytest-xdist` 3.8.0 and `execnet` 2.1.2). Every statistic and margin of a fast test comes from a throwaway script that is **not committed** (an **uncommitted lab measurement**). CI has not run on any of this.

### D-068 Results of the tier implementation
- **What was built, in commits.** `tests/tiers.py` (the data and `tier_of`); the hook in `tests/conftest.py` and the markers `fast` and `full` in `pyproject.toml`, with the unused `slow` marker removed (`rg 'mark\.slow'` found no use); `tests/test_tiers.py` (the guards); `pytest-xdist` with the pytester case that runs under `-n 2`; `tests/test_fast_integrators.py` and `tests/test_fast_physics.py`; one commit that turned two knobs of D-067 and made the guards cheaper; and `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. The lock gained only `pytest-xdist` and `execnet`, in the commit that first uses them (`git diff 6f553f2 HEAD -- uv.lock` names only those two packages and the two dev entries); `pyproject.toml` changed only by the dependency and the markers. No existing test module or helper, no file under `src/` or `experiments/`, and no `tolerances.py` changed (`git diff 6f553f2 HEAD --name-only` lists only the workflow, this log, `pyproject.toml`, `tests/conftest.py`, `uv.lock`, `tests/tiers.py` and the three new test files).
- **Counts.** The suite has 613 tests: the 539 old ones, with identical ids and identical collection order (a sorted diff of `--collect-only -q` before and after finds none missing and none changed), plus 74 new tests, all of them in the three new files (`tests/test_fast_physics.py` 36, `tests/test_tiers.py` 31, `tests/test_fast_integrators.py` 7). **The fast tier has 274 tests (the 200 old ones and the 74 new ones) and the full tier 339 node ids, all of them old**: the 33 full functions of D-067 and 17 functions times the 18 sets from `set-02` to `set-19`. `-m fast` and `-m full` have an empty intersection and their union is the whole collection; no new test is in the full list; no test was skipped or xfailed in any run.
- **Deviations from D-067, each with its reason.** (1) **K = 2, not 3** (knob 1), so the full tier holds 339 old tests and not 322, and the fast tier 200 old tests and not 217: the fast tier measured 31.5 s serial with K = 3 (below), over the budget of 30 s. `set-02` of the 17 functions moved to full. (2) **The reduced E1 test in `tests/test_fast_physics.py` runs on one chaotic-ish state, not two** (knob 2: the second chaotic-ish state is dropped from the reduced E1 test only; the E1 gate in `tests/test_physics_validation.py` is unchanged, with its seven states; corrected in D-070, which found that this entry said "the E1 gate test"); the looser-tolerance control still runs on the first two chaotic-ish states, as in the gate, and the damped control on the first regular and the first chaotic-ish state. The knobs of D-067 were turned in the stated order, and the later ones (the V1 regular states, the G2 states, the F record) were not needed. (3) **The expected cost of D-067 left out the guards**: `tests/test_tiers.py` is in the fast tier (the default) and costs about 2.7 s, the pytester case under `-n 2` about 1.4 s of it; the copy check of the F measurement (below) costs 0.2 s. (4) **The concurrency group of the workflow includes the event name** (`ci-${{ github.event_name }}-${{ github.ref }}`), so that a manual full run on a branch and the push-triggered fast run of the same branch do not cancel each other; the group and `cancel-in-progress` are otherwise kept. (5) **The full job runs serially**, because the xdist rule did not clearly say yes (below). Nothing else differs: no ceiling, tolerance, threshold, skip, xfail, timeout, warning filter, warm-up fixture or cache was added or changed.
- **How the F record length was handled.** `ph.fft_peaks` reads `tol.F_N_SAMPLES` inside and has no parameter for it, and the E1 helper `ph.dop853_energies` likewise reads `tol.E_HORIZON`, and `chk.measure_convergence` reads the gate's 12 states. **Chosen: re-express the few lines that depend on the size, in the new files, with the existing lower-level helpers** (`solve_reference`, `rk4_trajectory`, `energies_along`, `drift`, `spectrum_peaks`, `reference_pendulum`, `rk4_pendulum`, `log_log_slope`, `agreement_errors` and `dop853_round_trip_error`, which already take the size as an argument). The reason: it leaves the register and the gate machinery untouched and needs no patching in the measurement itself. `monkeypatch` is used in exactly one test, `test_the_reduced_f_measurement_is_the_gates_measurement`, as a check on the copy and not as a measurement: it sets `tol.F_N_SAMPLES` to 1000 for that test only (restored automatically), runs the gate's own `ph.fft_peaks` and the copy on the generic state with both integrators, and asserts that the peaks are identical (it passes).
- **Margins of every fast test and reduced control against its unchanged ceiling** (uncommitted lab measurement, `uv run --locked python` on the committed helpers, laptop, load about 3.7). Gate tests, ceiling divided by statistic (at least 3 is required): E1 at `t = 100` on regular-0 1.66e-12 (602 times), on chaotic-0 2.50e-11 (39.9 times); the second chaotic-ish state, now only in the rtol control, 2.24e-11 (44.6 times) when run as a gate; V1 regular on 3 states at `T = 20` 1.07e-12 (4,670 times); V1 chaotic-ish on 6 states at `T = 8` 6.21e-11 (805 times); F on 4,000 samples, worst of the 12 cases 2.165e-6 against `1e-5` (4.62 times; the others between 7.6e-7 and 2.2e-6); G3 on 6 states 3.617e-6 against `1e-4` (27.7 times; the worst state of the gate's 30 is state 0, with the same value, so it is among these 6); G4 on 3 states at `T = 20` 9.52e-9 against `1e-6` (105 times). G2 on 3 states: slopes 3.982, 3.957 and 3.995, the largest distance from 4 being 0.0433 (the allowed 0.0667 corresponds to 3.933 to 4.067), and the validity ratio, finest-step error over 30 times the reference's own, 22.0, 67.7 and 19.0 (at least 3 is required). Controls, statistic divided by ceiling (at least 3 is required): the damped field in E1 4,975 times on regular-0 and 95,269 times on chaotic-0; the looser reference tolerance in E1 **3.48 times on chaotic-0 and 4.41 times on chaotic-1** (the smallest margin of the set, as D-067 expected for `T = 100`); the damped field in V1 on 3 states at `T = 20` 963 times; F with `m2` times 1.01, 51.4 times; F with the wrong pair, 23,463 times. **Every margin holds**, so no knob was turned for a margin, and no ceiling was changed. These figures are for the first states of each gate's own ensemble, and they are not claims about the full ensembles.
- **Fast tier timing, serial** (three runs, `pytest -m fast -q -rA --durations=0`; the 1-minute load average just before each run in brackets; pytest's own clock, with the wall clock including `uv` in parentheses): 30.02 s (31.14 s; 3.20), 29.73 s (30.40 s; 3.33) and 29.77 s (30.84 s; 3.94), **median 29.77 s**. **The load was never below 3.2 in the whole session**, because other processes of the laptop were busy (the runs started under the threshold of 4, but the machine was not idle); so these figures are flagged as taken on a machine that was not calm, and one of the three runs is 0.02 s over 30 s. The first run with the D-067 sizes was 36.28 s (load 3.5; two later runs at that size, 47.24 and 50.19 s, started at load 3.9 and 5.2 and are not used); after the guards were made cheaper 31.48 and 31.76 s; with K = 2, 31.68 and 30.46 s; with the second E1 state dropped, 29.85, 29.67 and 29.85 s. The aim of 25 s is not met. The cost splits as `tests/test_fast_physics.py` about 10 s, the 200 old fast tests about 14.5 s, `tests/test_tiers.py` about 2.7 s and `tests/test_fast_integrators.py` about 1.9 s (sums of the `--durations=0` lines of two serial runs). The slowest fast test is `test_t1_mass_matrix_matches_the_oracle[set-00]` (the oracle build) with 1.85 s of its 60 s limit, so the worst fraction of the timeout in the fast tier is 0.031, and no phase comes near the guard's 15 s.
- **Fast tier under xdist** (`-n 2`, same command, three runs, interleaved with the serial ones): 16.70 s (17.11 s; 3.24), 17.15 s (18.05 s; 3.79) and 16.78 s (17.19 s; 3.38), **median 16.78 s, 43.6 percent faster than the serial median 29.77 s** (44.3 percent on the wall clock). **The rule of D-067 says yes: the fast job uses `-n auto`** (one worker per core; two on a two-vCPU runner, which is uncertain, because the runner's core count was not checked here). The three `-n 2` runs passed the same 274 node ids as the serial runs. The slowest fast test under `-n 2` takes 1.95 s. A run of the CI command with `-n auto` on this laptop (8 workers) had its slowest phase at 5.28 s (the first oracle test, with oversubscribed cores), which is why the guard of 15 s is not far above it at the runner's pace of up to 3.5 times (uncertain; a runner has fewer workers than this laptop).
- **Full tier, serial against `-n 2`** (three runs each, `pytest -q -rA --durations=0`, interleaved, load just before each in brackets; pytest's clock and the wall clock): serial 224.73 s (226.21 s; 3.92), 208.44 s (209.58 s; 3.62) and 227.62 s (228.83 s; 3.76), median 224.73 s (226.21 s); `-n 2` 128.21 s (129.16 s; 3.82), 197.36 s (197.88 s; 3.58) and 179.81 s (180.34 s; 3.77), median 179.81 s (180.34 s). **The median `-n 2` run is 19.99 percent faster on pytest's clock and 20.28 percent on the wall clock**, so the rule of D-067 (at least 20 percent) is met on one reading by 0.28 points and missed on the other by 0.01. The first `-n 2` run was 42.9 percent faster, and the other two were much slower, so the spread between identical runs (128 to 197 s) is larger than the gain's margin over the threshold. **Decision: the full job runs serially** (the rule is to use `-n` only if it clearly says so; uncertain), and a change to `-n auto` is one line in the workflow if the owner or Opus 5.5 prefers it. **The pass sets are equal**: all six runs and a seventh serial run at the final head had the same 613 passed node ids (sorted `-rA` lists compared), with no failure, error, skip or xfail; the fast tier's 274 ids are a subset of them. The serial full tier at the final head: 613 passed in 223.79 s (224.67 s wall), load 4.8 at the start (not calm).
- **Worst fraction of each timeout** (from `--durations=0`, setup plus call of one test against its own limit, over the six full runs): the E1 tests against 300 s, at most 0.076 (22.87 s) serial and 0.076 (22.71 s) under `-n 2`; the V1 regular test against 120 s, at most 0.126 (15.16 s) serial and **0.183 (21.99 s) under `-n 2`**; every other test against 60 s, at most 0.118 (7.09 s, the V1 chaotic-ish test) serial and 0.133 (8.00 s) under `-n 2`. Nothing is near 50 percent. The V1 regular test took 15.16 s in the first serial run, against about 11 s in the D-067 profile; the machine was busier.
- **The guards and proofs.** (a) Every item of the session has exactly one tier marker and it is the declared one (checked on the 613 items of a full run, and under `-n 2`): passes. (b) The pure checks on `tests/tiers.py` (K = 2, the 20 ids, the pinned counts 28, 3, 1 and 1, the 17 subset functions, the 339, 18 parametrized `tier_of` cases including the id `[F2-mode 2-dop853]`, a name that merely starts like a full function, and ids with brackets and `::`): pass. (c) Every full function exists with its pinned number of cases and each of the 17 families has the 20 ids `set-00` to `set-19`, exactly K fast and 20 minus K full (a subprocess `--collect-only`): pass. (d) `-m fast` and `-m full` select exactly the sets that `tier_of` declares, their union is the whole collection and their intersection is empty: pass; **in an uncommitted lab check, changing the hook to `trylast` made this guard and the hook guard fail**, which is the evidence that the guard can fail. (e) The hook applied to fake items adds exactly one of the two markers each and leaves the list unchanged in content and order, and it carries `tryfirst`: pass. (f) No other test module or helper mentions `mark.fast` or `mark.full`: pass. (g) A hung test fails with `Timeout (>1.0s)` inside an xdist worker (`[gw` lines in the output) and the session continues with the control test passing: pass. (h) `tests/test_timeout_config.py` passes, alone and under `-n 2`. Each guard takes well under the default 60 s, so no `@pytest.mark.timeout` override was added (D-036). The three `--collect-only` runs of the guards run side by side in threads, about a second each.
- **The workflow.** Read twice; triggers `push` on all branches, `pull_request` to `main` and `workflow_dispatch`; `permissions: contents: read`; the actions, uv version, Python version and runner as before. Job *fast tier (smoke)*: only on `push` to a branch other than `main`, 10 minutes, lint, format check, `pytest -m fast -n auto --durations=0 --durations-min=15` piped through `tee` under `bash` (so `pipefail` makes a pytest failure fail the step), then a step that fails if the saved log has any `call`, `setup` or `teardown` line with a duration (with `--durations-min=15` pytest lists only phases of 15 s or more; the 15 s ceiling and its reason are written in the workflow). Job *full tier (gates)*: on `pull_request`, `workflow_dispatch` and `push` to `main`, 20 minutes, lint, format check, `pytest --durations=15` with no `-m` and no `-n`. The syntax was parsed with the PyYAML of the system Python (not the project's environment, nothing was installed) and the structure printed. **The guard's `grep` was tried on the real output of the CI command on the laptop**: with the real threshold of 15 s it finds nothing and passes; with the threshold lowered to 1.5 s it finds lines and fails, so it can fire. The workflow itself was not run (no push is allowed here).
- **What could not be checked.** CI has not run: the fast job's time on the runner, the runner's core count, the full job's time, the duration guard in a real job and the behaviour of `-n auto` there are all unmeasured (the owner's go-ahead is needed to push, item (b) of D-066). The mutant run and the bitwise probe are dropped (D-066 addendum); the fast tier's ability to fail rests on its reduced controls for E1, V1 and F. **The reduced G2, G3 and G4 have no control** (their gates have none in the test files; milestone 3 relied on the mutation lab of D-050, which is not repeated here), so with the mutant run dropped nothing shows that the reduced G2 to G4 can fail: a known limit of the fast tier, which does not touch the guarantees, since those rest on the full tier (D-070, finding F2). The load of the laptop was never below 3.2, so no timing is from a calm machine.
- **Anything unexpected.** (1) The guards were not in the cost estimate of D-067 and cost about 2.7 s of a tier that is at the edge of 30 s; the median of 29.77 s has no margin, so a small addition to the fast tier can put it over and the next knob (V1 regular states from 3 to 2) is the first remedy. (2) The first serial runs were far above the D-067 figures (36 s and more) while the machine was busy: the fast-tier time moves by 20 percent or more with the load. (3) The xdist rule for the full tier landed on its threshold, within noise.

## Test tiers: the owner's decisions after the results (2026-10-15)

### D-069 Push, extensions, xdist in the full job, the merge and the scratch folders (the owner, with D-068 in front of them)
- **The owner's answers to five questions** (given on 2026-10-15; the first version of this entry, of the workflow comment and of `PROGRESS.md` dated them 2026-10-14, which was wrong, as the time of the commit that follows them, 08:10 on 2026-10-15, and of the push show; corrected in D-070): (1) **push the branch and open a draft pull request now**, so that CI runs the fast job (on the push) and the full job (on the pull request); (2) **keep both extensions beyond the owner's text**, the full tier on every push to `main` and the manual `workflow_dispatch` full run; (3) **use `-n auto` in the full job too**; (4) **the merge:** the agent merges locally with the noreply identity (D-064), pushes `main` and deletes the branch, remote and local, if Opus 5.5 gives an explicit `APPROVED FOR MERGE` and CI is green on both jobs with the fast job's test step under two minutes, and otherwise stops and reports; (5) **the lab's scratch folders outside the repository are deleted after the merge.**
- **What changes.** The full job now runs `pytest -n auto --durations=15` (the default `--dist load`), which reverses deviation (5) of D-068, where D-067's 20 percent rule had been applied literally. The measurements stand as they were: the laptop's `-n 2` run of the full tier was 19.99 percent faster than serial on pytest's clock and 20.28 percent on the wall clock (medians of three; identical `-n 2` runs spread from 128 to 197 s; an earlier, calmer lab run of D-067 gave about 31 percent). The owner chose `-n auto` for the full job on the evidence and the recommendation put to them (xdist was part of the request; the first CI run shows the real effect; going back is removing one option from one line). The workflow comment and the README say so. The conditions of D-066 for xdist stay: the pass set equals the serial one (613 passed on CI, and the local comparison of D-068); the seeded cases are identical across workers (xdist itself fails if workers collect different tests); and the worst fraction of each timeout under xdist is logged and reported above 50 percent (the laptop's worst under `-n 2` is 0.183, the V1 regular test against its 120 s).
- **The conditions of the merge (4) are conditions on the log too.** If the fast job's test step on the runner is two minutes or more, nothing is merged: the escalation of the D-066 addendum applies (`worksteal`, then the XLA and thread flags, then a persistent compilation cache), with the result logged, and the owner is told. If Opus 5.5 does not give the explicit approval, nothing is merged.

## Test tiers: the review of the pushed branch (2026-10-15)

### D-070 Opus 5.5's code review of the pushed branch, and what was done with it
- **State.** On the owner's go-ahead (D-069) the branch `test-tiers` was pushed at `e4e2caf` and draft pull request #5 was opened on 2026-10-15 at 08:11 local time; CI started at once, the push run (the fast job) and the pull-request run (the full job). Opus 5.5 reviewed `git diff 6f553f2 HEAD` read-only (it ran no test) and gave **`CODE APPROVED, PENDING CI`**, which is stage 1 of the final approval. The explicit `APPROVED FOR MERGE` is stage 2, to be given after the CI results and on the final head. By its reading, items 1 to 6 and 8 of the trimmed checklist hold (item 4 apart from the wording of F2), and item 7, the CI evidence, is open.
- **Findings, and what was done.** **F1 (required):** deviation (2) of D-068 said "the E1 gate test" where it meant the reduced E1 test; the gate is unchanged, with its seven states. **Corrected in D-068.** **F2 (required):** the reduced G2, G3 and G4 have no control (their gates have none in the test files, and milestone 3 relied on the mutation lab of D-050), so with the mutant run dropped nothing shows that they can fail; this is a known limit of the fast tier and does not touch the guarantees, which rest on the full tier. **Stated in D-068.** **F3 (recommended):** `cancel-in-progress: true` on the group of pushes to `main` lets a second push cancel the first one's full run, so that commit never gets a full run, which is the hole that the full run on `main` was meant to close. **Taken:** `cancel-in-progress` is now `${{ github.ref != 'refs/heads/main' }}`. It narrows the hole and does not close it, because GitHub keeps only one pending run per group, so the middle one of three quick pushes can still be dropped (that remark is the agent's, not Opus 5.5's). **F4 (non-blocking):** the E1 and G2 copies have no equality check against the gate's own helper like the F copy has. Cheap versions: patch `tol.E_HORIZON` to about 10 and compare `ph.drift(ph.dop853_energies(s))` with the reduced test's drift; patch `chk.regular_states` to a one-state slice and compare `chk.measure_convergence()` with the reduced G2 measurement. **Not taken:** they would add about 0.5 s to a fast tier whose median is 29.77 s against 30 s, and the owner scaled the work down; queued in `PROGRESS.md`. If the tier needs room, Opus 5.5 advises moving the pytester xdist proof (about 1.4 s, an infrastructure proof) to the full tier first. **F5 (note):** the reduced G2 stops at its first failing state, while the gate collects every violation (the same predicates; only the report differs). **The README now says** that pull requests to a base other than `main` get no CI.
- **Answers recorded.** (a) The 3 times rule for controls is **confirmed**, and the smallest control margin, **3.48 times** (the E1 looser-tolerance control on chaotic-0 at `T = 100`), is **the binding margin of the fast tier**: a platform difference of more than about 15 percent in DOP853's error at `rtol = 1e-10` would reach 3 times. If it ever fails on CI, the horizon of that control is lengthened to 200 (5.6 times) and the ceiling is never changed. (b) K = 2 and one E1 chaotic-ish state fewer in the reduced test: acceptable under the knobs. (c) The compile cache: acceptable; the fast tier's share of about 15 percent is an estimate and not a measurement; it stays the third remedy. (d) The budget is accepted as it is (29.77 s on a laptop whose load never fell below 3.2; the run 0.02 s over is noise; the real target is the CI step under two minutes) and no further knob is taken (the V1 knob would save about 0.15 s and weaken a gate's reduction). (e) The dropped items, the mutant run and the bitwise probe, are not needed for the guarantees; F2 is the only gap. (f) The workflow is sound apart from F3: `shell: bash` runs `bash -eo pipefail`, so a pytest failure fails the step through `tee`; the guard step is skipped when pytest fails; its pattern matches pytest's duration lines and not the "hidden" summary line; `branches: ["**"]` excludes tags; the event name in the group is right. Two things to watch in the first CI logs: under `-n auto` with two workers each builds the oracle (1.85 s on the laptop, about 6.5 s on the runner, perhaps 10 s under contention, against the 15 s of the guard; if the guard ever fires on that test it is reported and the 15 s is not raised), and `tests/test_tiers.py` under `--dist load` may run its module fixture in both workers (up to six concurrent `--collect-only` subprocesses on two vCPUs, 3 to 8 s each, against a 45 s cap and the 60 s limit; if it is over 15 s the guard says so).
- **Where Opus 5.5's advice differs from the owner's decision.** Opus 5.5 advised keeping the full job **serial** for this merge: D-067's 20 percent rule was declared in advance, and overruling it because its result landed on the threshold is a bad habit; it would re-decide from CI in a logged follow-up (switch to `-n auto` if the serial full job takes more than about 12 of its 20 minutes on any run). The owner decided otherwise on 2026-10-15, with the same numbers in front of them (D-069), and that decision stands: the rule was the agent's own heuristic, and the owner's choice is not a breach of it. Opus 5.5 is asked at stage 2 whether the choice blocks its approval; if it does, the question goes back to the owner.
- **What Opus 5.5 needs to see from CI for stage 2** (its list): (1) the head SHA, which includes F1, F2 and F3, the run ids, and green on both jobs; (2) the fast job: the test step's time and the job's time, the runner's core count (xdist's "created: N workers"), 274 passed, and the duration guard passed with no phase listed; (3) the full job: the test step's time and the job's time against 20 minutes, and 613 passed with no failure, skip, xfail or error; (4) the pass set: for a run under xdist, the count equals the collection (and xdist fails if workers collect different tests), with the laptop's serial `-rA` list as the reference; (5) the worst fraction of each timeout from the full job's `--durations` (E1 against 300 s, the V1 regular test against 120 s, the slowest other test against 60 s) and the slowest fast-tier phase against the guard's 15 s; (6) one full run on `main` after the merge.

## Test tiers: approval, merge and closing (2026-10-15)

### D-071 Final approval, merge and closing of the test-tier work
- **Opus 5.5's final approval (stage 2).** On the final head `26b0a64` it gave **`APPROVED FOR MERGE`** as the first line of its reply, after checking that `26b0a64` only changes documentation, the workflow's concurrency expression and comments, the README and `PROGRESS.md` (no test or code, noreply author) and that the CI facts below met every item of its list. Its answers to the three open points: (A) `-n auto` in the full job **does not block** (the owner's call, logged in D-069 with its dissent in D-070; it carries no guarantee risk, since the pass count equals the collection, xdist reported no collection mismatch and the seeded cases are deterministic; its speed on the runner is unproven, not harmful); (B) V1 regular at 55.3 percent of its 120 s: **report only, no action** (the timeout was declared in advance, 66.38 s leaves 1.8 times on a VM that was uniformly slow, and changing it after one observation is what D-025 and D-036 exist to prevent); (C) **no objection** to merging on the two samples of the fast job (93 s against 120 s).
- **The owner's conditions for the merge (D-069) were met:** Opus 5.5's explicit `APPROVED FOR MERGE`; CI green on both jobs of the final head; the fast job's test step under two minutes (93 s).
- **CI on the final head `26b0a64`** (Ubuntu 24.04, Python 3.12.3, jax and jaxlib 0.11.2, numpy 2.5.3, scipy 1.18.1; lint and format passed). **Fast job** (push run 38758849550): `274 passed in 89.20s`; the test step 93 s against the 120 s target; the whole job 107 s; xdist `created: 2/2 workers`, so the runner has two cores; every setup, call and teardown phase under 15 s (822 durations hidden) and the duration guard passed. **Full job** (pull-request run 38758859829, `-n auto`): `613 passed in 587.05s (0:09:47)`, no failure, skip or xfail; the test step 592 s and the whole job 603 s, half of the 20-minute limit. **On the first pushed head `e4e2caf`** (runs 38758085824 and 38758097878) the fast job took 90.25 s of pytest (a 93.7 s step, a 112 s job) and the full job 188.81 s (207 s in all). **After the merge, on `main`** (run 38760990009, push event, head `90bd70a`, not cancelled, the fast job skipped as intended): `613 passed in 358.10s (0:05:58)` under `-n auto` with 2 workers, the whole job 6 min 16 s. **The three full runs of the same suite under `-n auto`, 188.81 s, 587.05 s and 358.10 s of pytest, show the runner's speed spreading by 3.1 times** (the old serial runs spread by 3.5 times); the heavy E1 and V1 tests slowed together, so it is the VM and not the code. The fast job showed no spread in two samples, which is too few to say that there is none.
- **The worst fractions of each timeout, under xdist on the runner.** On the slowest full run (the final head): E1 gates 66.66 s of 300 s (22.2 percent) and E1 controls 40.78 s of 300 s (13.6 percent); **V1 regular 66.38 s of its 120 s (55.3 percent, above Opus 5.5's reporting line of 50 percent)**; V1 chaotic-ish 27.96 s of 60 s (46.6 percent); G4 25.13 s of 60 s (41.9 percent); every other test at most 17.34 s. On the run on `main`: V1 regular 43.82 s (36.5 percent), E1 43.71 s (14.6 percent), V1 chaotic-ish 18.40 s (30.7 percent), G4 16.30 s (27.2 percent). Nothing failed or timed out. The fast job's slowest phase is under 15 s; its exact value is not in the log.
- **The merge.** Pull request #5 was merged locally, at the owner's pre-authorization, with the noreply identity (D-064): merge commit `90bd70a` (parents `6f553f2` and `26b0a64`; author and committer `172058618+adb228@users.noreply.github.com`, checked before the push; the tree is identical to the branch head), pushed to `main`; GitHub shows the pull request as merged at 15:30:51 UTC (08:30 local). The branch `test-tiers` was deleted on the remote and locally. The repository is still private; the agent did not change it.
- **The scratch folders** of the measurement run were deleted after the merge, as the owner decided: the standalone clone `wm-lab-fasttier` (474 MB), `wm-lab-out` (4.2 MB, the raw outputs and scripts behind D-067) and a few files in `/tmp`, about 481 MB in all. The numbers they held are in D-067 and D-068 as uncommitted lab measurements; the raw outputs no longer exist, and no script was ever committed (as in D-038).
- **Follow-ups queued** (none is a condition on the merge; Opus 5.5's list, also in `PROGRESS.md`): (1) the speed of `-n auto` in the full job is unproven on the runner: on one of the next full runs a serial run is compared and logged, and `-n auto` is kept only if it is not slower than serial there (this needs a small workflow change, an input of `workflow_dispatch` that selects serial, which is not made yet); (2) the fast step's time is recorded for the next few pushes, and if it ever reaches 120 s the first remedy is a serial-against-`-n auto` comparison of the fast job on the runner (with two vCPUs, per-worker startup and the per-worker oracle build may cost more than parallelism saves), ahead of the escalation of the D-066 addendum; (3) if a later run puts the V1 regular test above 75 percent of its timeout (90 s), a new declared value is proposed through D-036 before that test next runs, and the timeout is not simply raised; (4) the full run on `main` for the merge commit is confirmed above (completed, not cancelled, 613 passed); (5) F4, the equality checks of the E1 and G2 copies (D-070); (6) the binding margin of the fast tier, 3.48 times (the E1 looser-tolerance control), and the remedy that D-070 records if it ever fails on CI.
- **What the work delivered.** The 539 tests of `main` are unchanged and the full tier runs all of them, with 74 new tests; the fast tier (274 tests, a smoke tier and never a gate) takes about 30 s serial on the laptop and, on the runner, a test step of 93 s (1 min 47 s for the whole job; two samples); the gate files, `src/`, `experiments/` and `tolerances.py` are unchanged by the work; no tolerance, threshold, skip, xfail or timeout was added or changed; `pytest-xdist` and `execnet` are the only new packages. The decisions are D-066 to D-071.

## Claude Code setup: the owner's answers to the handoff questions (2026-10-15)

### D-072 Reviewer, trailer, permissions and the full job under Claude Code (Owner)
Not sent to a reviewer: it concerns the owner's accounts and the reviewer's own setup. The agent wrote this entry and the three files that follow it (`CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/agents/reviewer.md`, `.claude/settings.json`).
- **Choice, reviewer.** A read-only Opus subagent (`.claude/agents/reviewer.md`) with fresh context for each review. The two reviews of the deferral rule (D-025) stay: the design before code and the diff after CI is green. The explicit line `APPROVED FOR MERGE` stays the requirement for a merge. Alternatives: the owner reviews; a separate session that carries the log. Why: a fresh context is independent of the implementer's, and the Cursor reviewer cannot be resumed.
- **Choice, trailer.** Keep Claude Code's default `Co-Authored-By` trailer on new commits. This adds the address `noreply@anthropic.com` to the history, so the history scan of D-065 must allow it, as it allows the old Cursor trailer; the scan itself is not changed here.
- **Choice, permissions.** Allowed without asking: tests, Ruff, commits and pushes on a branch, draft pull requests, docs-only pushes to `main`, and merging when CI is green and the reviewer has approved, then deleting the branch. Ask the owner first: CI or workflow changes, gate or tolerance changes, anything account-level. Never: force-push. Everything else of D-021, D-033.3, D-064 and D-065 stands (merge locally with `--no-ff` and the noreply identity, no history rewrite, the repository's visibility is the owner's, no tag without approval, the brief is edited only on the owner's instruction).
- **Choice, the full job.** Keep `-n auto` and drop the serial comparison. This supersedes follow-up (1) of D-071 and the item in `PROGRESS.md`; D-071 is pushed and is not edited.
- **What the settings enforce, and what they cannot.** `.claude/settings.json` allows the routine commands, asks for edits under `.github/workflows/` and to `src/horizon/tolerances.py`, asks for pushes to `main`, tags and `gh` account commands, and denies force-pushes, rebase, amend, `gh pr merge` and `gh repo edit`. It cannot test "docs-only", "CI is green" or "the reviewer has approved", so a push to `main` still prompts and those conditions are written in `CLAUDE.md` and in the reviewer's prompt. If the owner prefers no prompt, the rule moves to the allow list.
- **Next.** Milestone 5 starts in a separate session with the math walkthrough (D-039); nothing of it is in this change.

## Claude Code setup: corrections after the review of pull request #6 (2026-10-16)

### D-073 What D-072 overstated, and the fixes (Owner)
Not sent to a reviewer for the same reason as D-072; the real `reviewer` agent re-reviews the result once the owner has restarted the session. The agent wrote this entry and the commits that follow it. D-072 is pushed and is not edited: this entry corrects it.
- **State.** `reviewer` could not be addressed by name in the session that created it (agent definitions load at session start), so a fresh Opus subagent followed `.claude/agents/reviewer.md` and reviewed `git diff main...80a7f74`. CI on that head was green (push run 38830210691: `274 passed in 60.58s`; pull-request run 38830215431: `613 passed in 595.99s`, both under `-n auto` with 2 workers). Its verdict was `CHANGES REQUIRED`: one required finding (R1), four recommended (R2 to R5) and seven nits (N1 to N7). Nothing was merged.
- **What D-072 overstated.** (1) "Denies force-pushes, rebase, amend": the deny patterns match `--force` and `--force-with-lease` anywhere but `-f` only as the first or last argument, `+refspec` only unquoted, and `--amend` only as the first argument; they miss `git push origin -f main`, `-uf`, `--mirror`, a quoted `"+main"` and `git commit -m x --amend`, and the broad `Bash(git push:*)` allow lets those run without a prompt. The earlier dry-run test exercised only the `--force` pattern. (2) "A push to `main` still prompts": only two spellings did; bare `git push` on `main`, `git push -u origin main`, `origin HEAD:refs/heads/main` and `origin <branch>:main` did not. (3) "Read-only" reviewer: it had Bash, and the project's allow rules apply to a subagent, so it could commit, push and run tests; it was read-only by instruction only. (4) "The conditions are in the reviewer's prompt": the conditions for a merge are in `CLAUDE.md`; the reviewer's file has the CI condition for its own verdict only. (5) Not stated in D-072: `git merge` is not allowed by the settings, so the local merge prompts; and `git branch -f` or `-D` and `git fetch +src:dst` rewrite local refs and are not denied (publishing them needs a push).
- **Choice, what guards what.** "Never force-push" is guarded by the instruction (`CLAUDE.md`) and by a **GitHub ruleset on `main`** that blocks force-pushes and deletion; the deny list in `.claude/settings.json` is a backstop, and glob patterns cannot catch every spelling (combined short flags, quoted refspecs). **The ruleset is account-level and the owner sets it up themselves; it is not in place when this entry is written**, and whether this repository's plan supports it while it is private is not checked here. Alternative considered: a PreToolUse hook as the hard guard; not taken (a new tool needs its own entry, and the ruleset also covers pushes made outside Claude Code).
- **Choice, the settings.** Deny added for `-f` anywhere, `--mirror`, `--amend` anywhere, and for deleting `main` (`--delete main`, `:main`, `:refs/heads/main`); deleting other remote branches stays allowed, since D-072 allows deleting the merged branch. Ask added for bare `git push`, any push whose last argument is `main` or ends in `:main`, `refs/heads/main`, `--tags` and `refs/tags/`. Ask added for edits to the frozen gate files (R5): `src/horizon/tolerances.py`, `tests/cases.py`, `tests/test_tolerances.py`, the milestone 2 to 4 test files (`test_double_pendulum.py`, `test_oracle_double_pendulum.py`, `test_integrators.py`, `test_integrator_gates.py`, `test_physics_validation.py`), the helpers `tests/*_checks.py` and `tests/oracles/**`, and, by pattern, the register and gate files of later milestones (`tests/test_*_register.py`, `tests/test_*_gates.py`). A new file that matches those patterns also prompts when it is created, which is intended for a milestone's gate files. Paths now start with `/` to anchor them at the project root (N1; the anchoring in this version is not verified). The routine rule is `git push origin <branch>`; a bare `git push` prompts.
- **Choice, the reviewer.** Tools `Read, Grep, Glob` only; no Bash. The main session writes the review input to a file in its scratchpad directory (never committed): the head SHA, `git diff main...<head>`, the commit identities (`git log --format` with author and committer e-mail), and for every CI run its id, event, job conclusions, test counts, timings and the xdist worker count, and passes the file's path in the prompt. The reviewer can read the working tree at the head but cannot check CI itself, and says what it relied on. A new reviewer is started for each review; an earlier one is never continued (N7).
- **Other changes.** `PROGRESS.md` lines that contradicted D-072 are corrected (the merge and the branch deletion, the account list, the branch flow, the serial comparison in Next), the allowlist of e-mail addresses has one canonical copy, in `CLAUDE.md`, and the next free number is D-074. `CLAUDE.md` gains the nits N3, N5, N6 and N7; `reviewer.md` gains N4 and its new input. A pending item is added: re-run the D-065 scan (it is a `git` and `rg` procedure whose commands are recorded nowhere) before the owner changes the repository's visibility, and log the commands then.
- **Not done.** N2 is left as it is (the local merge prompts; local ref rewrites are not denied). Whether the matcher's trailing ` *` also matches the bare command, and the path anchoring, are not tested.

"Rulesets" stays in the list of account-level things that need the owner's approval: it is a permission, not a guard.

Whether that lab run came before or after the change of visibility is not recorded. D-065's check of a word in the tree takes its words from the variable `HISTORY_SCAN_WORDS` and is skipped when that is unset; the descriptions of pull requests are not scanned (they are not in git; the lab run of 2026-10-16 found no e-mail address, no local path and no token-like string in those of pull requests #1 to #6). The script excludes itself from the tree and diff sections, because its patterns would match themselves. Author and committer names are printed as they are; only addresses are masked. It is read-only (`git status` runs with `--no-optional-locks`), needs `git`, `grep`, `sed`, `awk` and `perl`, no new dependency, and nothing runs it in CI.
